Research conducted 2026-07-25/26. Each section is a research agent's verbatim report, with its own source URLs and its own unverified-item flags. **Read the flags** — several sections correct premises that turned out to be wrong (e.g. Litera does not own CaseMap; LexisNexis does).

5. Legal AI & case-narrative — Harvey/Hebbia, CoCounsel/Clearbrief, CaseMap/Litera
6. AI-native criminal-defense startups — JusticeText, Matey, Casefleet, Reduct, NexLaw, Supio
7. Commoditization threat — GraphRAG, frontier labs, NotebookLM, Neo4j

# 1. Graph & link-analysis platforms

## 1.1 Siren

I have comprehensive coverage. Compiling the final research report.

# Competitive Research: Siren (siren.io) — as of July 2026

*Note on naming: siren.io (Siren, formerly "Siren Solutions"/Kibi) is distinct from Siren Analytics (sirenanalytics.com) and Siren Associates (Lebanon-focused NGO contractor) — both surfaced in searches and are unrelated companies.*

---

## 1. Company overview

Siren is a VC-backed "investigative intelligence" software company headquartered in Galway, Ireland (PorterShed), founded in 2014 by Giovanni Tummarello and Renaud Delbru as a spinout of big-data/semantic-web research at NUI Galway's DERI (now Insight Centre); John Randles (ex-Eontec, ex-PolarLake/Bloomberg) has been CEO since 2017 ([ThinkBusiness founding profile](https://www.thinkbusiness.ie/articles/siren-intelligent-investigations-tech/), [Advertiser.ie](https://www.advertiser.ie/galway/article/144875/siren-a-leading-force-in-investigative-intelligence-technology)). Its platform sits on top of Elasticsearch and sells search + link-analysis + AI to law enforcement, national security, and financial-crime teams. Scale: "over 70 employees" per a Galway profile ([Advertiser.ie](https://www.advertiser.ie/galway/article/144875/siren-a-leading-force-in-investigative-intelligence-technology)); third-party trackers put it in the 51–100 band ([startupintros](https://startupintros.com/orgs/siren)) — I could not verify a precise 2026 headcount. Funding history: ~€10M Series A (Nov 2019; DVI Equity Partners, Frontline Ventures, Atlantic Bridge, Enterprise Ireland), €12M European Investment Bank quasi-equity/venture debt (Feb 2023) ([Silicon Republic](https://www.siliconrepublic.com/start-ups/siren-funding-cybersecurity-intelligence-funding-eib-galway), [EIB press release](https://www.eib.org/en/press/all/2023-072-cybersecurity-firm-siren-accelerates-momentum-with-eur-12-million-eib-backing)), and a **strategic investment from Elastic (NYSE: ESTC) announced 2 Dec 2025** (amount undisclosed) — no acquisition or ownership change; still independent and private ([Siren/Businesswire](https://siren.io/siren-secures-investment-to-advance-ai-driven-investigations-for-national-security/), [Businesswire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251202354147/en/Siren-Secures-Investment-to-Advance-AI-Driven-Investigations-for-National-Security)).

## 2. AI/LLM capabilities (2025–2026)

- **Siren AI plugin (v1.2→2.0)**: integrates LLMs into Siren Investigate. Providers: OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, AWS Bedrock, Ollama, and (in 2.0) any OpenAI Chat Completions-compatible endpoint — i.e. bring-your-own-model, including local models via Ollama. Features: **K9 Companion** chat assistant, **graph-report generation** ("generate comprehensive reports by analyzing user-created graphs"), and LLM methods exposed in Siren's scripting API for custom GenAI workflows; docs include permissions and auditing sections ([Siren AI 1.4 docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-ai/1.4/siren-ai/c_introduction.html), [Siren AI 2.0 docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-ai/2.0/siren-ai/c_introduction.html)).
- **Siren 14.7 (1 Jul 2025)**: flagship "AI-driven standard for global policing" release — use "any LLM directly within Siren" to generate reports, summarize findings, and interact with data in natural language; explicitly markets **"traceable AI interactions"** for accountability/ethics ([Siren 14.7 press release](https://siren.io/siren-14-7-new-ai-driven-standard-for-global-policing/), [Officer.com](https://www.officer.com/command-hq/technology/computers-software/investigation-software/press-release/55300806/siren-147-sets-a-new-ai-driven-standard-for-global-policing)).
- **K9 "Loyal AI Companion for Investigators" (launched 17 Sep 2025)**: conversational natural-language querying of link charts; **one-click automated investigative workflows** (e.g., burglary/trafficking case launches that auto-gather suspects, incidents, connections); translation, evidence summarization, structured report drafting. Heavy emphasis on **explainability: every AI action "auditable, traceable, and backed by verifiable data references"; investigators verify conclusions with a single click; the system flags uncertainty ("teaching K9 when to say no")** ([Siren K9 press release](https://siren.io/siren-introduces-k9-the-loyal-ai-companion-for-investigators/), [Help Net Security](https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2025/09/17/siren-k9-ai-for-law-enforcement-agencies/)). The June 2025 GQL announcement describes K9's roadmap as "intelligent agents" built on the new query foundation ([GQL press release](https://siren.io/siren-adopts-iso-standard-gql-to-power-the-next-generation-of-graph-intelligence/)).
- Not verified: exact citation UX mechanics (docs pages for K9 returned 404 in my checks), whether K9 is fully agentic multi-step vs. tool-assisted chat, and any per-token/AI pricing.

## 3. Graph / link analysis and data model

Siren does **not** use a native graph database. Its core IP is **Siren Federate**, an Elasticsearch plugin adding distributed **join/semi-join** capability: joins run on an in-memory distributed compute layer across the ES cluster; semi-joins manipulate compact doc-ID bitsets (cacheable), and a "Connector" module exposes remote backends (JDBC databases, other ES clusters) as **virtual indices**. The knowledge graph is therefore a **virtual/logical graph**: a data model of entity tables + relations declared over indexes, traversed at query time via joins — records stay where they are ([Federate intro docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-federate-user-guide/36/siren-federate/introduction.html), [Federate architecture docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-federate-user-guide/38/siren-federate/architecture.html), [Elastic blog](https://www.elastic.co/blog/investigative-analysis-of-disjointed-data-in-elasticsearch-with-the-siren-platform), and Siren's own 2025 arXiv paper ["Siren Federate: Bridging document, relational, and graph models for exploratory graph analysis"](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.07815)). Front-end: the **Graph Browser** in Siren Investigate (link charts, shortest path, drag-and-drop from dashboards, map projection of graph data) ([Investigate docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-platform-user-guide/15.0/siren-investigate/siren-investigate.html)). In **June 2025 Siren adopted ISO-standard GQL** as a unified query language spanning full-text search, relational queries, and graph traversal — claiming complex investigations dropped "from hours to minutes" ([GQL press release](https://siren.io/siren-adopts-iso-standard-gql-to-power-the-next-generation-of-graph-intelligence/)). Siren 14.7 added **entity resolution** (auto-merge of equivalent records with lineage preserved) and **"records-as-relations"** (modeling calls/transactions as first-class relation records) ([14.7 release](https://siren.io/siren-14-7-new-ai-driven-standard-for-global-policing/)).

## 4. Evidence ingestion — documents and phone extractions

- **Unstructured documents**: yes, via the **Siren NLP plugin** — an Elasticsearch ingest pipeline doing named-entity extraction (Person, Organization, Location, plus taxonomy terms) over emails/web content/free text, multi-language; extracted entities are auto-matched for link visualization ([Siren NLP docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-nlp/0.5.1/siren-nlp/introduction.html)). So the graph over unstructured text is semi-automatic: NLP annotates, the data model + entity resolution links.
- **Cellebrite: yes, native connectors since Siren 12 (Nov 2021)** — "Siren Cellebrite Connectors" ingest **UFED XML data dumps and Cellebrite Pathfinder deployments**: calls, contacts, emails, IM, journeys, social media, sensors; per-device deduplicated party/contact lists; NER over content; and crucially **fusion of phone-extraction identifiers with agency data (CDRs, CAD/RMS, watchlists) in one model** — exactly the "one graph over phone + documents" pattern. Availability is restricted "only to qualifying organizations" ([Siren Cellebrite connector post](https://siren.io/mobile-phone-data-fusion-with-the-siren-cellebrite-connectors/)).
- **GrayKey/Magnet**: no GrayKey-specific connector found, but the **March 2026 Magnet Forensics partnership** brings Magnet-acquired digital evidence (Magnet One / Magnet Review, via Magnet One Connect API) into Siren — Magnet owns GrayKey, so this effectively covers that ecosystem ([Siren–Magnet press release](https://siren.io/new-strategic-partnership-between-siren-and-magnet-forensics-to-unify-digital-evidence-with-investigative-intelligence/), [Magnet One evolution](https://www.magnetforensics.com/news/magnet-forensics-redefines-digital-investigations-with-evolution-of-magnet-one/)).

## 5. Deployment model

**Self-hosted software, not SaaS.** Docs describe ZIP packages (Windows/Linux/macOS), Docker images, Windows/Linux services, and Kubernetes via ECK, always against a customer-run Elasticsearch/Federate cluster; no managed-cloud offering appears anywhere in the install docs ([setup docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-platform-user-guide/15.0/siren-investigate/setting-up-siren-investigate.html)). Air-gapped operation is not explicitly documented, but the posture strongly supports it: on-prem stack, **Ollama/local-LLM support in Siren AI** ([Siren AI docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-ai/1.4/siren-ai/c_introduction.html)), and a July 2026 Ocient partnership pitched at **"sovereign national security missions"** ([press-release archive](https://siren.io/category/press-release/)). A **free Community Edition** has existed since Siren 10.2 (1 node, 1 concurrent user, 500-node graph cap, 10 dashboard relations, forum-only support) alongside paid **IT** and **Business** editions with per-node pricing ([editions page](https://siren.io/siren-platform-editions/), [10.2 announcement](https://siren.io/siren-platform-10-2-released/)); the current downloads page still offers free platform downloads and a "Technical Free Trial" but no longer foregrounds the "Community Edition" branding — current status of that tier is **partially unverified** ([downloads](https://siren.io/downloads/)).

## 6. Go-to-market and pricing

Sales-led enterprise motion: "request a demo / request pricing" everywhere; **no public price points found**. Channels: **Carahsoft** for US government (federal/state/local contract vehicles; no published prices) ([Carahsoft Siren page](https://www.carahsoft.com/siren), [contracts](https://www.carahsoft.com/siren/contracts)), **DataExpert** in Europe (forensics-focused reseller) ([DataExpert](https://www.dataexpert.eu/news/the-power-of-the-ai-in-siren/) — see their [Siren AI page](https://www.dataexpert.eu/news/the-power-of-ai-in-siren/)), and a SoftwareOne marketplace listing ([SoftwareOne](https://platform.softwareone.com/vendor/siren/VND-3482-4971)). I found **no AWS/Azure Marketplace listing and no public procurement award with a disclosed Siren Platform price** (searches surfaced only unrelated "siren" hardware tenders). **Could a 15-person PI firm or small defense practice buy it?** Technically yes at the free/community tier (self-hosting Elasticsearch + Siren, 1 user, capped link analysis), but the commercial product is per-node, sales-negotiated, aimed at agencies/enterprises, and the Cellebrite connectors are gated to "qualifying organizations" ([Cellebrite connector post](https://siren.io/mobile-phone-data-fusion-with-the-siren-cellebrite-connectors/)) — realistically it is not a self-serve purchase for a small firm, and running/administering the stack requires real IT capacity. This is a clear gap Loupe can exploit.

## 7. Target buyers

Primary: **law enforcement and intelligence agencies** (border security, counter-narcotics, anti-trafficking, counterintelligence) ([LE&I page](https://siren.io/law-enforcement-and-intelligence/)); **national security**; **financial crime/fraud/AML and compliance teams**; **cyber threat intelligence**; and general **enterprise search** (e.g., Apollo.io as a commercial customer, Jan 2025) ([press-release archive](https://siren.io/category/press-release/), [Elastic investment release](https://siren.io/siren-secures-investment-to-advance-ai-driven-investigations-for-national-security/)). Marketing "with mission-critical deployments at the world's largest police departments" ([14.7 release](https://siren.io/siren-14-7-new-ai-driven-standard-for-global-policing/)). **No evidence of any focus on defense attorneys, law firms, or private investigators** beyond a generic "commercial investigators" mention in the K9 release — Loupe's legal-defense/small-practice segment is not addressed.

## 8. Investigator-judgment constructs (collections, hypotheses, narratives, contradiction detection)

Siren's durable objects are **tool-artifacts, not judgment-artifacts**: saved searches, dashboards (exportable/shareable/embeddable), saved graphs in the Graph Browser, and jsreport-based report templates ([Investigate user guide](https://docs.siren.io/siren-platform-user-guide/15.0/siren-investigate/siren-investigate.html), [dashboards docs](https://archive.docs.siren.io/siren-platform-user-guide/14.6/siren-investigate/dashboard.html)). There are **timeline visualizations** (Timeline/Timelion time-series, temporal analysis across disconnected sources) ([Investigate product page](https://siren.io/siren-investigate/)), and K9/Siren AI can **generate narrative reports from graphs** ([Siren AI docs](https://docs.siren.io/siren-ai/2.0/siren-ai/c_introduction.html)). But I found **no dedicated "Investigation"/"Case" object in the current user-guide navigation, no hypothesis-testing workflow, no contradiction/disconfirmation detection, and no automatic narrative timeline** feature. The closest things: K9's uncertainty-flagging ("when to say no") and one-click case-type workflows ([K9 release](https://siren.io/siren-introduces-k9-the-loyal-ai-companion-for-investigators/)), navigation history for retracing investigative steps (14.7), and alerting/watch capabilities. Durable, evidenced collections capturing investigator reasoning — the "Loupes" concept — appear to be white space.

## Unverified / flagged items

- Precise current headcount and revenue (only "70+" from a 2024-era local profile; 51–100 per trackers).
- Elastic investment amount (undisclosed).
- Whether the free "Community Edition" tier still exists under that name (editions page still lists it; downloads page no longer uses the term).
- Air-gapped deployment is inferred (on-prem + Ollama + "sovereign" positioning), not explicitly documented.
- K9 detailed docs (citation UX, agent architecture) — doc URLs 404'd; description rests on press releases and Siren AI plugin docs.
- Any deal sizes/pricing — nothing public found on Carahsoft, marketplaces, or procurement records.

**Competitive takeaways for Loupe**: Siren is the closest architectural analogue (search-backed virtual graph + NLP ingest + Cellebrite fusion + explainable AI assistant) but is aimed squarely at large agencies with self-hosted Elasticsearch infrastructure, gated forensic connectors, and sales-led per-node pricing. It has no defense-bar/small-practice motion, no turnkey single-tenant offering for small teams, and no durable narrative/hypothesis/contradiction constructs — those remain differentiators for Loupe.

## 1.2 Linkurious

# Competitive Deep Research: Linkurious (linkurious.com) — as of 2026-07-26

## 1. Company overview

Linkurious SAS is a graph-intelligence / "decision intelligence" software vendor founded January 2013 in Paris by Sébastien Heymann, David Rapin, and Jean Villedieu — Heymann co-created the open-source Gephi graph tool ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linkurious)). HQ is at 66 Rue Marceau, Montreuil (greater Paris), with a US office in Bethesda, MD. Headcount is ~51 employees as of mid-2025 ([LeadIQ](https://leadiq.com/c/linkurious/5a1d9dda23000053008dc276)). The company was essentially bootstrapped — Crunchbase records only ~$55K raised (2014) ([Crunchbase](https://www.crunchbase.com/organization/linkurious)) — and reached ~€7M annualized contract value (June 2025) with positive EBITDA in FY2024 ([Nuix announcement](https://www.nuix.com/news/nuix-acquires-graph-intelligence-leader-linkurious)). **Major ownership change: ASX-listed Nuix Limited acquired Linkurious for €20M** (€12.5M cash + €2.5M Nuix equity + €5M earnout tied to ACV growth/cross-sell over 24 months), signed 3 Dec 2025 ([Nuix](https://www.nuix.com/news/nuix-acquires-graph-intelligence-leader-linkurious), [MarketScreener](https://www.marketscreener.com/news/nuix-limited-signed-an-agreement-to-acquire-linkurious-sas-for-20-million-ce7d51dfd98df52d)), completed **20 April 2026** ([completion notice](https://linkurious.com/blog/nuix-linkurious-acquisition-completion/)). The ICIJ role is verified: ICIJ used Linkurious (via the free "Linkurious for Good" NGO program) as the link-analysis front end for the Panama Papers, Paradise Papers, FinCEN Files, Swiss Leaks and Pandora Papers ([Linkurious blog on Pandora Papers](https://linkurious.com/blog/technology-pandora-papers-investigation/), [ICIJ](https://www.icij.org/investigations/panama-papers/data-tech-team-icij/), [Linkurious for Good](https://linkurious.com/linkurious-for-good/)).

## 2. AI/LLM capabilities (2025–2026)

**Important correction to the brief:** there is no "Linkurious Enterprise v5.x." The current line is **v4.3.x** (v4.3.10 released 10 July 2026) ([release notes](https://doc.linkurious.com/admin-manual/latest/release-notes/)).

- **Query AI** is the flagship GenAI feature: natural-language → Cypher query generation. Announced ~Feb 2026, GA in v4.3.0 ([Query AI blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/query-ai-graph-data-natural-language/), [4.3 blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/linkurious-enterprise-4-3/)). BYO-LLM: OpenAI/ChatGPT, Gemini, Claude, Mistral, plus Azure OpenAI and Amazon Bedrock (added in 4.3.x point releases), or a fully self-hosted custom model. Privacy posture: **only the graph schema is sent to the LLM, never data**. Generated Cypher is human-reviewable, editable, and saveable. Extended to Amazon Neptune in v4.3.6 ([release notes](https://doc.linkurious.com/admin-manual/latest/release-notes/)).
- That is the extent of the LLM story. **No evidence found of**: an answer-synthesizing copilot/chat over case data, RAG over documents, AI-generated narrative summaries, or citation/provenance of AI answers. Query AI generates queries, not answers — provenance is implicit (you run the query yourself). Could not verify any deeper AI assistant; flagging as not found.
- "Entity Resolution AI" is marketed as AI but is **Senzing** under the hood (partnership, no-code interface, explainable match decisions) — not LLM-based ([partnership announcement](https://linkurious.com/blog/linkurious-and-senzing-join-forces/)). The platform also claims "65+ graph and AI algorithms" (community detection, risk scoring, anomaly detection) ([platform page](https://linkurious.com/platform/)).

## 3. Graph/link-analysis capability & data model

- Linkurious Enterprise is a **web application layered on top of an external graph database** — it is not itself a database. Supported backends: **Neo4j, Memgraph, Azure Cosmos DB, Amazon Neptune, Google Cloud Spanner, Google BigQuery Graph** ([platform page](https://linkurious.com/platform/), [release notes](https://doc.linkurious.com/admin-manual/latest/release-notes/)). Search layer requires Elasticsearch 8+ as of 4.3.
- Visualization is their own commercial **Ogma** WebGL JavaScript library (also sold standalone as a dev SDK; claims thousands of nodes interactive, GPU layouts) ([Ogma page](https://linkurious.com/ogma/), [Ogma docs](https://doc.linkurious.com/ogma/latest/)).
- Modular platform: **Unify & Enrich** (graph DB + Senzing ER + analytics), **Detect** (graph-powered **alerts + case management** — cases group related alerts, unified case list, shared-entity detection across alerts), **Analyze & Decide** (visualizations, no-code query builder, Query AI, collaboration) ([platform](https://linkurious.com/platform/), [banking solution](https://linkurious.com/banking-solution/)).

## 4. Evidence ingestion

- **Requires an already-populated graph.** The model is: you (or a partner/SI) ETL your data into Neo4j/Neptune/etc., then Linkurious connects, indexes, visualizes, and alerts on it. The only native ingestion added recently is a **CSV import** (new in 4.3, positioned for "rapid prototyping") ([4.3 blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/linkurious-enterprise-4-3/)). No unstructured-document → graph pipeline exists in the product; in the Pandora Papers, ICIJ did document processing with its own stack (Datashare, custom ETL, Neo4j) before Linkurious entered the picture ([Pandora Papers tech blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/technology-pandora-papers-investigation/), [ICIJ](https://www.icij.org/investigations/panama-papers/data-tech-team-icij/)).
- **No Cellebrite UFED / GrayKey handling found anywhere** — no integration, connector, or marketing mention (searched; nothing verifiable). Phone-extraction ingestion would be entirely DIY ETL.
- The Nuix acquisition exists precisely to fill this gap: Nuix Neo does large-scale unstructured/forensic data processing, and the stated rationale is combining it with Linkurious graph/visualization for "financial crime detection, compliance, and complex investigations" ([Nuix](https://www.nuix.com/news/nuix-acquires-graph-intelligence-leader-linkurious)). A future Nuix-forensic-data → Linkurious-graph pipeline is the obvious roadmap risk to watch, but no shipped integration was found as of July 2026.

## 5. Deployment model

Both: (a) **self-managed** on-prem or customer-cloud — the full "Decision Intelligence Platform" with ER, alerts, case management, API, plugins, SSO/SAML/LDAP; (b) **managed SaaS** ("Linkurious Enterprise Cloud"), hosted by Linkurious in EU data centers — but this tier **excludes** entity resolution, alerts, case management, API and plugins ([pricing page](https://linkurious.com/pricing/)). Single-tenant on-prem is their heritage (banks, law enforcement).

## 6. Go-to-market & pricing

- **Published pricing exists for the cloud tier only: €490/user/month** (8% off yearly, 30-day free trial) ([pricing page](https://linkurious.com/pricing/)). The full self-managed platform is quote-only, sales-led ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/145640/Linkurious/) confirms no public plans for the enterprise product). No reliable public deal-size data found (flagged unverified); €7M ACV across a customer base "across North America, Europe, APAC" suggests mid-five to six-figure enterprise deals.
- **Could a 15-person PI firm or small defense practice buy it?** Technically yes for the cloud tier (~€5.9K/user/yr; 3 seats ≈ €17K/yr, self-serve trial). Practically, poorly suited: the cloud tier strips out alerts/case management, and any tier presupposes the customer can stand up and populate a graph database — there is no evidence pipeline, no document processing, no phone-extraction handling. It's a graph front end, not an evidence platform. Post-Nuix ownership will likely push it further upmarket toward Nuix's enterprise/government motion.

## 7. Target buyers

Financial institutions (AML/CTF, fraud — e.g., Ria Money Transfer case study), law enforcement & intelligence agencies, cybersecurity, supply-chain risk, and government ([banking](https://linkurious.com/banking-solution/), [law enforcement](https://linkurious.com/law-enforcement-investigation-solution), [AML](https://linkurious.com/aml-investigation-solution/)). Journalists/NGOs (ICIJ, Justice for Myanmar, The Sentry) are served via the free **Linkurious for Good** program — i.e., a brand halo, not a revenue segment ([Linkurious for Good](https://linkurious.com/linkurious-for-good/), [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linkurious)). NASA uses it for its Lessons Learned database. Partners include PwC, Deloitte, Capgemini, Neo4j, Microsoft, AWS ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linkurious)). **No evidence of law firms or private investigators as a served segment.**

## 8. Collection/narrative objects & investigator-judgment capture

Closest analogues, per the [user manual](https://doc.linkurious.com/user-manual/latest/):
- **Saved visualizations** — durable, auto-saved, shareable, publishable, duplicable ("duplicate a selection into a new visualization" added in 4.3); organized into **"Spaces"** (folders/workspaces).
- **Comments and annotations** on visualizations; collaborative editing.
- **Case management** — cases as containers grouping related alerts, with statuses and a unified case list; cross-alert shared-entity surfacing ([banking solution](https://linkurious.com/banking-solution/)).
- **Timeline** — a temporal *filter* component on visualizations (plus geospatial mode), not an auto-generated narrative timeline.

**Not found (flagged):** hypothesis-testing workflows, contradiction/disconfirmation detection, automatic narrative/timeline generation, evidence-cited AI narratives, or any report-writing feature. The saved-visualization + comment model captures investigator judgment only as manually curated pictures — the closest thing to Loupe's "collection/narrative" concept, but with no evidentiary provenance chain and no generative layer.

## 9. Notable news 2025–2026

- **Jun 2025** — Linkurious Enterprise 4.2: no-code query builder GA, node group collapsing ([4.2 blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/linkurious-enterprise-4-2/)).
- **3 Dec 2025** — Nuix signs agreement to acquire Linkurious, €20M ([Nuix](https://www.nuix.com/news/nuix-acquires-graph-intelligence-leader-linkurious), [Linkurious](https://linkurious.com/blog/nuix-signs-agreement-to-acquire-linkurious/), [DLA Piper advisory note](https://www.dlapiper.com/en-us/news/2025/12/dla-piper-advises-nuix-on-strategic-acquisition-of-ai-decision-platform-linkurious)).
- **Feb 2026** — Query AI announced (NL-to-Cypher) ([blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/query-ai-graph-data-natural-language/)).
- **20 Apr 2026** — acquisition completed; Nuix combined-entity ACV guidance A$252–272M ([completion](https://linkurious.com/blog/nuix-linkurious-acquisition-completion/)).
- **Jul 2026** — Linkurious Enterprise 4.3 (Query AI GA, CSV import, French/Japanese UI, Azure OpenAI/Bedrock support) ([4.3 blog](https://linkurious.com/blog/linkurious-enterprise-4-3/), [release notes](https://doc.linkurious.com/admin-manual/latest/release-notes/)).
- No layoffs, additional funding rounds, or other M&A found.

## Competitive takeaways for Loupe

1. **Linkurious is a graph front end, not an evidence platform** — it presumes a populated graph DB and has zero document or phone-extraction ingestion. Loupe's ingest-to-graph pipeline (Cellebrite, discovery docs) is a categorical differentiator today.
2. **Their AI stops at query generation** (schema-only, BYO-LLM) — no answer synthesis, no cited AI narratives, no agentic investigation. Loupe's cited-LLM-agent layer is well ahead of shipped Linkurious capability.
3. **The Nuix acquisition is the threat vector**: Nuix Neo (forensic/unstructured processing, including experience with mobile data) + Linkurious graph could converge on Loupe's territory for enterprise/government buyers within 12–24 months — no shipped integration yet.
4. **They do not serve small firms**: sales-led full platform, €490/user/mo for a stripped cloud tier, no legal-defense or PI positioning. The small/mid investigative-practice segment Loupe targets is effectively open.
---
## 1.3 Maltego

# Competitive Deep Research: Maltego (as of 2026-07-26)

## 1. What it is, ownership, scale

Maltego Technologies GmbH (Munich, Germany; founded out of Paterva's Maltego tool, company est. 2008/2017) is a link-analysis and OSINT investigation platform plus a curated data marketplace. Its core product renders investigations as node-link graphs where "Transforms" (API queries) pivot from one entity (person, email, domain, phone, crypto wallet…) to related entities across 120+ integrated data providers ([maltego.com](https://www.maltego.com/), [pricing page](https://www.maltego.com/pricing/)).

- **Ownership**: Boston PE firm **Charlesbank Capital Partners** acquired Maltego from Maxburg Capital Partners in April 2023 via its Technology Opportunities Fund, committing **over $100M** for product development, international expansion, and follow-on acquisitions ([Maltego blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/maltego-secures-100m-to-accelerate-growth-of-its-intelligence-platform-to-combat-cybercrime-and-misinformation/), [Charlesbank](https://www.charlesbank.com/news/maltego-secures-100m-to-accelerate-growth-of-its-intelligence-platform-to-combat-cybercrime-and-misinformation/), [Kirkland & Ellis](https://www.kirkland.com/news/press-release/2023/04/kirkland-advises-charlesbank-capital-partners-on-acquisition-of-munich-based-maltego), [Mergr](https://mergr.com/transaction/charlesbank-capital-partners-acquires-maltego-technologies)).
- **Scale**: 100+ employees at time of the 2023 acquisition (Kirkland press release); claims **200K+ users worldwide, 2K+ government organizations, 4K+ private companies, 60%+ of Dow 30**, ISO 27001:2022 certified ([maltego.com](https://www.maltego.com/)). Leadership: CEO **Philip Mayrhofer**, CTO **Ben April** (Maltego blog/LinkedIn).
- **Acquisitions**:
  - **PublicSonar** (real-time social/news crisis monitoring, NL) and **Social Network Harvester** (social profile collection/analysis, from German firm MH Service) — both **March 2024**, positioning "all-in-one investigation platform" ([Maltego blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/maltego-acquires-publicsonar-and-social-network-harvester/), [Forensic Focus](https://www.forensicfocus.com/news/maltego-acquires-publicsonar-and-social-network-harvester-to-propel-vision-of-an-all-in-one-investigation-platform/)). PublicSonar became **Maltego Monitor**; SNH underpins **Maltego Evidence** ([DataExpert](https://dataexpert.eu/products/osint-platforms-maltego/maltego-monitor)).
  - **Hunchly** (web-capture/evidence-preservation browser tool) — **May 2025** ([Maltego blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/maltego-welcomes-hunchly-to-expand-osint-capabilities/), [OSINT News](https://osint-news.com/2025/05/maltego-acquires-online-evidence-preserving-tool-hunchly-to-expand-osint-capabilities/)). Hunchly is now bundled from the €3,000/yr tier up ([pricing](https://www.maltego.com/pricing/)).
- **Social Links**: I found **no evidence of an acquisition**. Social Links (sociallinks.io) remains an independent vendor whose SL Professional product is sold as a Maltego Transform pack/partnership ([sociallinks.io](https://sociallinks.io/products/sl-professional/maltego)). **Could not verify** any 2025–26 change in that relationship — flag as partnership, not acquisition.

## 2. AI/LLM capabilities (2025–2026)

Maltego's AI story is real but modest — an assistant layered on OSINT products, not an agentic reasoning engine:

- **AI Assistant** — a conversational assistant integrated into **Maltego Graph (Browser) and Maltego Search**. Documented capabilities: "reading, analyzing, and reporting on information on the graph," data cleaning, tagging, translation, and general investigative advice; it has a usage-quota system ([Maltego docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000061164-what-is-the-ai-assistant-)).
- **Maltego One** (launched **Oct 27, 2025**) — the new unified browser platform — ships with "an AI Assistant to help investigators work faster and smarter," initially scoped to Person-of-Interest investigations ([Maltego blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/introducing-a-new-investigation-platform-maltego-one/)).
- **Maltego Monitor** uses ML/NLP for signal recognition and **AI-powered sentiment analysis** of social streams ([maltego.com](https://www.maltego.com/), [Maltego Monitor](https://www.maltego.com/monitor/)); marketing also claims AI-assisted analysis of images/video/large text volumes.
- **Not verified / apparently absent**: which underlying LLM is used, **citation/provenance of AI answers**, hallucination controls, or any natural-language-to-Transform query language. The docs overview explicitly doesn't cover model, privacy, or source-citation behavior — I could not verify that Maltego's AI cites evidence at all. The Maltego Search product page makes **no explicit AI claims** ([maltego.com/search](https://www.maltego.com/search/)). This is a meaningful contrast point for Loupe's cited, evidence-grounded answers.

## 3. Graph/link-analysis capability and data model

- **Model**: typed **Entities** on a canvas; **Transforms** are server-hosted functions that take an entity and return related entities/edges. The graph is an analyst working document, not a persistent case knowledge graph — data lives where the connectors query it, and the graph stores what the analyst pulled ([Building Integrations docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000053545-building-integrations-for-maltego)).
- **Clients**: Maltego Graph desktop (Java) is the classic tool; **Maltego Graph (Browser)** and now **Maltego One** move link analysis into the browser with built-in data views (map, histogram) and guided Transforms ([Maltego One blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/introducing-a-new-investigation-platform-maltego-one/)).
- **Server products**: public **TDS** (Transform Distribution Server) routes Transform calls; **iTDS** is the on-prem equivalent for proprietary data (Docker/VM deployable, Python `maltego-trx` library); **CTAS** is an on-prem pack of Maltego's standard Transforms using customer-owned API keys; a **Comms** add-on enables shared-graph collaboration ([TDS/iTDS docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000020198-what-is-a-transform-distribution-server-tds-itds-), [CTAS docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000020187-what-is-a-maltego-standard-transforms-on-premise-server-ctas-), [deployment overview](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000059973-overview-of-deployment-options)).
- **CaseFile** (the old offline evidence-mapping client) was discontinued as a separate product; its offline features folded into Maltego Graph ≥4.8 Community Edition ([search-result summary of Maltego docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000036759-maltego-products-and-plans)).

## 4. Evidence ingestion (documents, phone extractions)

**This is Maltego's biggest gap versus Loupe.**

- **Structured import only**: the built-in Graph Import Wizard ingests **CSV/Excel tables** (map columns to entities, auto-draw links) ([Import Graph docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000010797-import-graph-3rd-party-table)). Unstructured documents require **external preprocessing** — community blog posts describe DIY pipelines (LLM-extract entities to CSV, then import, or wrap it in a custom Python Transform) ([OSINT Team blog](https://osintteam.blog/turn-any-document-into-a-maltego-graph-29477bedea8c)). There is no native "drop a discovery PDF and get a graph" capability.
- **No native Cellebrite UFED/GrayKey support found**: I found no Maltego connector, importer, or partnership for mobile forensic extractions; forensic-tool interop searches surface Everlaw, MOBILedit etc. instead ([e.g., Everlaw's UFDR upload docs](https://support.everlaw.com/hc/en-us/articles/360051062431-Upload-Cellebrite-Exports)). Flagged as **verified-absent to the limit of public documentation** — an org could hand-roll a UFDR→CSV→import pipeline or custom iTDS Transform, but nothing is productized.
- **What "Evidence" actually means at Maltego**: Maltego Evidence captures and preserves **public social-media data** (profiles, posts, chat messages on open platforms, media) with automated screenshots and chain-of-custody logging, stored locally ([maltego.com/evidence](https://www.maltego.com/evidence/), [Evidence docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000058628-introduction-to-maltego-evidence)). Hunchly adds analyst-driven web-page capture ([Hunchly blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/maltego-welcomes-hunchly-to-expand-osint-capabilities/)). Neither ingests legal-discovery corpora or phone dumps.

## 5. Deployment model

- Desktop client (Graph) + browser SaaS (Search, Monitor, Evidence web, Maltego One), with **Community Cloud** (public Transform server) by default; **Enterprise Cloud** offers dedicated EU/US servers and single-customer dedicated deployments ([deployment options doc](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000059973-overview-of-deployment-options)).
- **On-prem**: iTDS/CTAS servers (VM image or Docker Compose) keep Transform traffic and proprietary data inside customer infrastructure ([iTDS docs](https://docs.maltego.com/en/support/solutions/articles/15000020198-what-is-a-transform-distribution-server-tds-itds-)). Offline graph work is possible (ex-CaseFile features), and Evidence stores data locally, but **a fully air-gapped end-to-end deployment is not documented publicly** — OSINT connectors inherently need internet. **Could not verify** any formally supported air-gapped bundle.

## 6. Go-to-market and pricing

Hybrid self-serve + sales-led, with public prices at the low end ([maltego.com/pricing](https://www.maltego.com/pricing/)):

- **Basic (free)**: Graph Community Edition, 200 credits/mo (1,000 for gov/org emails).
- **Entry Standard**: **€3,000/yr**, 10K credits/mo, includes Hunchly.
- **Professional**: **€7,500/yr per seat** (up to 5 users; Standard 20K / Advanced 40K credits), unlimited Graph + Search + commercial data access. (The older "~$5k/yr Professional" figure is stale; current list is €7,500.)
- **Enterprise**: contact-sales; 5+ users/government; adds optional Monitor and Evidence, premium data, training.
- Data is metered by a **credit system** via the **Maltego Data Pass** (bundled curated OSINT + commercial data: breach data, social profiles, email/domain lookups), replacing much of the old per-vendor Transform-Hub purchasing ([pricing](https://www.maltego.com/pricing/)).
- **Could a 15-person PI firm or small defense practice buy it?** Yes, financially — free tier to trial, €3K–7.5K/yr self-serve card-purchasable tiers. But Monitor/Evidence are Enterprise-gated, and the product presumes OSINT skills (entity/Transform mental model), pivot-based workflows, and pay-per-credit data. For defense-discovery work (their evidence is *received*, not *collected from the open web*) Maltego offers essentially no ingestion path, so it functions as an adjacent OSINT tool, not an evidence platform.

## 7. Target buyers

Government, Defense & National Security, **Law Enforcement** (FBI, INTERPOL cited as users), Cyber Threat Intelligence, Banking, Insurance, corporate security/trust & safety; secondary audiences include journalists and independent investigators via the free/entry tiers ([maltego.com](https://www.maltego.com/), [Toolkitly profile](https://www.toolkitly.com/maltego)). **Law firms and defense practices are not a named vertical anywhere I found** — the persona is the analyst/investigator collecting external data, not counsel working produced discovery.

## 8. Investigator-judgment / narrative / hypothesis features

Thin, and mostly evidentiary-capture rather than reasoning:

- **Maltego Cases** appears on the pricing grid as cloud case storage with encryption and collaboration ("Limited" in low tiers, "Full" in Professional/Enterprise) ([pricing](https://www.maltego.com/pricing/)) — case containment, not narrative objects. **Could not verify** deeper capabilities (public docs are sparse).
- Chain-of-custody and audit logging exist in **Evidence** (screenshots, evidence reports, compliant usage logging) and **Hunchly** (timestamps, metadata, defensible capture) ([maltego.com/evidence](https://www.maltego.com/evidence/), [Hunchly blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/maltego-welcomes-hunchly-to-expand-osint-capabilities/)).
- Graph annotations/bookmarks and one-click PDF/report exports exist ([reporting blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/five-ways-to-export-maltego-graph-for-reporting/)).
- **No evidence of**: durable narrative/collection objects capturing investigator judgment, hypothesis-testing workflows, contradiction/disconfirmation detection, or automatic narrative timelines (Evidence has activity-pattern timelines for social data only). Nothing resembling Loupe's planned "Loupes" concept was found.

## 9. Notable news 2025–2026

- **Oct 27, 2025** — **Maltego One** unified browser platform launched (POI-investigation focus, AI Assistant; deeper product-suite integration promised "into 2026 and beyond") ([blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/introducing-a-new-investigation-platform-maltego-one/)).
- **May 19, 2025** — **Hunchly acquired** ([blog](https://www.maltego.com/blog/maltego-welcomes-hunchly-to-expand-osint-capabilities/)).
- **Apr 17, 2025** — Frost & Sullivan 2025 Product Leadership Award, Global OSINT ([company news](https://www.maltego.com/categories/company-news/)).
- **Mar 17, 2026** — thought-leadership post on investigative-platform evolution; no product/corporate announcements found in 2026 H1 ([company news](https://www.maltego.com/categories/company-news/)).
- **No layoffs, leadership changes, or new funding rounds found** for 2025–26 (absence of evidence, not proof of absence).

## Unverified / flagged items

1. **Social Links**: partnership only; no acquisition found; current 2025–26 status of the partnership unverified.
2. **AI Assistant internals**: underlying model, data privacy, and especially **whether AI answers carry citations/provenance** — not publicly documented.
3. **Air-gapped deployment**: on-prem Transform servers exist, but no documented fully air-gapped offering.
4. **Cellebrite/GrayKey ingestion**: no public evidence of native support (verified absent in public docs/marketing).
5. **Maltego Cases** depth: only pricing-grid references found.
6. Employee count is as of the 2023 acquisition (100+); current headcount unverified (third-party trackers like Tracxn/LeadIQ suggest low hundreds but were not directly confirmed).

**Competitive takeaway for Loupe**: Maltego is an outbound-OSINT collection-and-pivot platform monetizing a data marketplace, now consolidating into a browser SaaS with a light AI assistant. It does not ingest discovery documents or phone extractions, has no defense-legal vertical, no evidence-grounded/cited AI answers documented, and no narrative/hypothesis constructs — the spaces where Loupe differentiates. Its threats to Loupe are brand ubiquity in investigations, a 120+-source data layer, and a credible push (Maltego One, 2026 roadmap) toward an "all-in-one investigation platform."

## 1.4 DataWalk

All research complete. Here is the full report.

# DataWalk — Competitive Deep Research (as of 2026-07-26)

Prepared as competitive input for Loupe (AI-native investigative evidence platform for small/mid legal-defense and investigative practices).

---

## 1. What it is, ownership, scale

DataWalk is a "unified Graph + AI investigations platform" for financial-crime and public-safety analytics — it fuses large, disparate datasets into a no-code knowledge graph for link analysis, entity resolution, and pattern detection, sold to government agencies, law enforcement, and banks ([datawalk.com/company](https://datawalk.com/company/)). Founded ~2010–2011 in Wrocław, Poland as PiLab S.A. by Paweł Wieczyński (CEO EMEA), Krystian Piećko (CTO), and Sergiusz Borysławski; a Delaware entity (DataWalk Inc., US CEO Gabe Gotthard) was added in 2016. US HQ is now Redwood City, CA (2000 Broadway St), European HQ Wrocław ([datawalk.com/company](https://datawalk.com/company/), [Silba Deep Dives analysis](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)). Listed on the Warsaw Stock Exchange (DAT.WA / DAT PW).

**Financial scale** (per DM BOŚ brokerage coverage under the WSE Analytical Coverage Support Program — the most authoritative public source):
- FY2024 revenue: **PLN 24.6M (~USD 6M), -4% yoy**; FY2025 revenue grew **+53% yoy** (≈ PLN 37.6M, ~USD 9–10M) — below management's 70% target ([DM BOŚ Feb 5, 2026 report](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf); [DM BOŚ Mar 31, 2026 note](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)). *Flag: a secondary source ([ad-hoc-news.de](https://www.ad-hoc-news.de/boerse/news/ueberblick/datawalk-s-a-stock-pldatwl00015-ai-driven-data-analytics-innovator/69328034)) claims 2024 revenue of PLN 28.5M +15%, and the Silba substack claims PLN 13.5M — both conflict with BOŚ; I treat BOŚ as authoritative.*
- Still loss-making: 2025E net loss ≈ **PLN -57M** (heavily inflated by non-cash incentive-program costs; adjusted EBITDA ≈ PLN -13.5M). "Rule of 40" reached +10 in 2025, positive for the first time in 3 years. 4Q25 revenue PLN 6.7M, **-26% yoy** ([BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- Cash PLN 57.7M at end-2025, plus a new share issuance booked in 1Q26; prior Series R+S raises totaled ~PLN 84M (Series S 2025: PLN 58M) ([BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf), [Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)).
- Market cap **~USD 263M** at PLN 151.48/share (Feb 2026); 12M range PLN 56–175; free float 82%; founders control ~27% of votes via FGP Venture ([BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf), [Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)).
- Headcount: **~38 FTE** plus a large contractor/equity-holder network (per [Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration), ~mid-2025 — could not verify against filings; genuinely tiny relative to Palantir either way). Customer base ~36 paying customers, with heavy Rabobank concentration (~71% of Q1 2025 revenue) — same source, flag as unaudited.

**Bottom line on scale:** a ~USD 10M-revenue, ~40-person, loss-making but well-capitalized company with an outsized federal footprint.

## 2. AI/LLM capabilities (2025–2026)

DataWalk's AI story is **"Composite AI" + knowledge-graph grounding of LLMs**, not a chat-first product:

- **LLM grounding / Graph-RAG**: the platform augments LLMs (GPT-4 via API, Llama, and locally hosted models) with the customer's structured+unstructured data; positions the knowledge graph as the anti-hallucination "persistent context layer" ([datawalk.com/solutions/generative-ai](https://datawalk.com/solutions/generative-ai/), [hallucination-trap article](https://datawalk.com/why-generative-ai-in-banking-requires-a-persistent-context-layer/)).
- **MCP Server** (marketed as "New" as of mid-2026; exact release date unverified): exposes DataWalk querying, scoring, entity resolution, and graph capabilities to external AI agents via Model Context Protocol. Security pitch: "badge, not a master key" — agent requests execute under the calling user's existing DataWalk permissions, with full audit logging. Supported LLMs: OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, Llama, local endpoints; client platforms: Claude, Copilot, Cursor, LangGraph, Azure AI Foundry, Vertex AI. Deployable on-prem/private-cloud/air-gapped. Use cases: alert triage, **SAR drafting**, AML/KYC enrichment, LE investigations ([datawalk.com/solution/mcp-server](https://datawalk.com/solution/mcp-server/)).
- **Natural-language querying**: "plain language" investigative questions through the MCP route and familiar AI tools; agents can decompose questions into sequenced tasks against the DataWalk API, no-code ([MCP server page](https://datawalk.com/solution/mcp-server/), [generative-ai page](https://datawalk.com/solutions/generative-ai/)).
- **Citation/provenance**: AI summary responses "include links to the documents upon which the summary response was generated" ([generative-ai page](https://datawalk.com/solutions/generative-ai/)). This is document-link-level provenance, not per-sentence citation as far as public materials show.
- **Composite AI** (CTO Piećko's framing, 2025): knowledge graph + inference engine (rule-based derived relationships with incremental refresh) + ML (scikit-learn/XGBoost/PyTorch, in-database feature engineering) + graph algorithms (PageRank, embeddings, community detection) + NLP/LLM via MCP + vector search ([Composite AI article](https://datawalk.com/datawalk-composite-ai-unified-enterprise-ai-for-intelligent-business-solutions/)).
- **LLM-generated report summaries** in law-enforcement/defense contexts ([law-enforcement page](https://datawalk.com/industries/law-enforcement/), [defense page](https://datawalk.com/industries/defense/)); anti-pure-LLM positioning content, e.g., "why LLMs fail at UBO identification, how agentic AI delivers accuracy" ([UBO article](https://datawalk.com/ubo-identification-why-llms-fail-how-composite-ai-delivers-accuracy/)).

**Not verified:** precise release dates/version numbers for any GenAI feature; no evidence of an autonomous end-to-end "AI investigator" agent — the model is human analyst + governed agent access to the graph.

## 3. Graph/link-analysis capability & data model

- **Hybrid graph-relational engine, not a native graph DB**: "a single graph/relational database" that "seamlessly integrates the strengths of graph and relational databases," combining RDF-style ontology flexibility with labeled-property-graph scale plus OLAP (pivots, slicing) and vector search in the same store — i.e., a set/columnar-relational core presenting as a graph, exactly as your brief expected ([knowledge-graph page](https://datawalk.com/product/knowledge-graph-software/), [product page](https://datawalk.com/product/), [Composite AI article](https://datawalk.com/datawalk-composite-ai-unified-enterprise-ai-for-intelligent-business-solutions/)). Piećko holds ~18 patents on this; a new **US patent was granted Jan 15, 2026** ([Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration), [BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **"Universe" model / Universe Viewer**: the visual, no-code query interface over object sets and relationships; "no-code data fusion & ontology," visual queries/rules/scores, multi-hop queries, virtual paths and inferred (indirect) connections ([product page](https://datawalk.com/product/), [Cellebrite Pathfinder comparison](https://datawalk.com/cellebrite-pathfinder/)).
- **Scale claims**: "billions of records" processing capability, insights "up to 10× faster than legacy approaches" ([defense page](https://datawalk.com/industries/defense/)); Silba describes "billions of digital records simultaneously" ([Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)).
- Entity resolution is a first-class, no-code component ([entity-resolution solution](https://datawalk.com/solutions/entity-resolution/), [product page](https://datawalk.com/product/)).

## 4. Evidence ingestion (documents + phone extractions)

- **Unstructured documents**: yes — NLP/LLM pipeline for entity extraction, entity resolution, text summarization, multilingual translation, and object detection to enrich the knowledge graph from unstructured sources ([generative-ai page](https://datawalk.com/solutions/generative-ai/), [defense page](https://datawalk.com/industries/defense/)). Automatic graph building from documents is claimed at the marketing level ("AI and a knowledge graph automate data import, linking, and analysis" — [law-enforcement page](https://datawalk.com/industries/law-enforcement/)); depth of automation is not documented publicly.
- **Mobile phone extractions**: the law-enforcement page lists **"device data and mobile phone extractions"** and CDRs among ingestible sources ([law-enforcement page](https://datawalk.com/industries/law-enforcement/)), and DataWalk runs an aggressive **competitive page against Cellebrite Pathfinder** claiming it can fuse data from "unlimited devices" without Pathfinder's silo limits ([datawalk.com/cellebrite-pathfinder](https://datawalk.com/cellebrite-pathfinder/)). **However: I found no Cellebrite/GrayKey partnership, no documented native UFED/UFDR/GrayKey format connector, and no technical documentation of extraction-format support** — searches surfaced nothing beyond the marketing claims (the notable Cellebrite legal-tech partnership is with Relativity, not DataWalk — [Cellebrite/Relativity news](https://www.stocktitan.net/news/CLBT/relativity-and-cellebrite-partner-to-unleash-the-power-of-mobile-6l6qj78625ci.html)). Treat "phones + documents natively in one model" as positioning that likely relies on services-led ETL, not turnkey ingestion. **This is a genuine gap vs Loupe's native Cellebrite ingestion.**

## 5. Deployment model

- **On-prem / private cloud / hybrid / air-gapped**, explicitly including classified environments: "engineered for open, classified, and fully isolated (air-gapped) environments," runs on commodity hardware, agencies retain "full operational sovereignty" with no forward-deployed engineers ([defense page](https://datawalk.com/industries/defense/), [MCP server page](https://datawalk.com/solution/mcp-server/)). "Agency-owned system" framing for law enforcement ([law-enforcement page](https://datawalk.com/industries/law-enforcement/)).
- **No multi-tenant SaaS offering found.** This is single-tenant, deployed software — implementation averages ~19 weeks per Silba ([Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)).
- **FedRAMP: no authorization found** in any material reviewed (contract-vehicles page lists none; a search snippet mentioned only a vague "FedRAMP compliance path" I could not trace to a primary source — treat as unverified/absent). Federal sales go through channel primes instead: GSA Schedule GS-35F-284DA via Research Innovations Inc (RII), DOJ JMD BPA #15JPSS20F00000524 (RII prime), NASA SEWP, NIH CIO-CS, Army ITES-SW2 via DLT Solutions, plus a Canadian SLSA ([datawalk.com/contract-vehicles](https://datawalk.com/contract-vehicles/)).

## 6. Go-to-market & pricing — could a small firm buy it?

- **Sales-led enterprise motion with services**, long cycles: government 24–36 months, banks 6–12 months ([Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)). New VP Worldwide Sales Peter Sofarelli (ex-Quantexa/SymphonyAI/Tibco/SAS) hired to scale this motion; company explicitly moved to "larger, higher-margin contracts" and a "new upgraded price list" in the subscription model ([BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf), [BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **Price points**: shifted from ~$300K perpetual licenses to subscriptions starting around **$560K/year** (Silba, flag as unaudited but directionally consistent with reported deal math). Example deals: **Rabobank ~USD 10M over 5 years** (1Q25); Morgan Stanley (Nov 27, 2025); Polish National Police HQ (Nov 13, 2025); Amazon + Polaris Wireless (4Q24); DOJ MNF/SIFT continuously since 2019 under the **$500M DOJ analytics BPA** ([BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf), [BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf), [DOJ BPA page](https://datawalk.com/datawalk-included-in-500m-bpa-at-doj/), [DOJ retention release, Feb 4 2026](https://datawalk.com/datawalk-retained-by-the-u-s-department-of-justice-to-power-landmark-financial-crime-investigations/)). Positioning vs Palantir: "~70% of the capability at 20–30% of the price" ([Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration)).
- **Verdict for Loupe's market: no.** A 15-person PI firm or small defense practice cannot realistically buy DataWalk — six-figure annual minimums, ~19-week services-led implementation, no self-serve, no SaaS, no published pricing, procurement via federal channel partners. DataWalk is structurally absent from Loupe's segment.

## 7. Target buyers

Law enforcement, intelligence agencies, defense, government/public-sector investigations, banking & payments (AML/fraud/KYC), insurance fraud, eCommerce/retail fraud ([datawalk.com/company](https://datawalk.com/company/), industry pages: [law-enforcement](https://datawalk.com/industries/law-enforcement/), [defense](https://datawalk.com/industries/defense/), [banking](https://datawalk.com/industries/banking/), [insurance](https://datawalk.com/insurance-fraud-detection-software/)). Named/claimed customers: US DOJ (MNF/SIFT), DoD, DHS/State (logos), Rabobank, Morgan Stanley, Barclays, ING, Ally Financial (fraud case study), Amazon, Polish National Police, Polish Ministry of Finance, TUiR Warta, Kooperativa. **Notably absent: legal defense, litigation/discovery, private investigators — the prosecution/agency side only.**

## 8. Narrative/hypothesis/contradiction features (Loupe-relevant)

- **Hypothesis testing**: claimed only at the level of "rapid hypothesis testing" via agile no-code querying — analysts can quickly generate and check hypotheses against the graph ([From Chaos to Investigative Context, Oct 27 2025](https://datawalk.com/from-chaos-to-investigative-context/), [knowledge-graph page](https://datawalk.com/product/knowledge-graph-software/)). No formal hypothesis objects, no competing-theories management.
- **Contradiction/disconfirmation detection: nothing found.** No feature detecting evidence that contradicts a theory.
- **Automatic narrative timelines**: "Timeline & Case Management" is listed among law-enforcement analysis tools ([law-enforcement page](https://datawalk.com/industries/law-enforcement/)) and LLM summaries can draft reports, but there is no automatic narrative-timeline generation from evidence in public materials.
- **Durable collection/narrative objects capturing investigator judgment: nothing found.** The October 2025 "investigative context" article explicitly stops at unified data context; it does not describe capturing analyst reasoning, curated evidence collections with provenance of judgment, or narrative artifacts ([article](https://datawalk.com/from-chaos-to-investigative-context/)).
- **This whole cluster — Loupe's "Loupes" concept — is white space relative to DataWalk.** Their inference engine produces derived *relationships* (rules over the graph), not durable investigator-authored narrative objects.

## 9. Notable news 2025–2026

- **Feb 4, 2026** — DOJ retains DataWalk for the Money Laundering, Narcotics & Forfeiture program (SIFT), multi-year extension via prime RII; DataWalk in this role since 2019 ([press release](https://datawalk.com/datawalk-retained-by-the-u-s-department-of-justice-to-power-landmark-financial-crime-investigations/)).
- **Jan 15, 2026** — new US patent granted ([BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **Nov 2025** — Morgan Stanley contract (Nov 27) and Polish National Police HQ contract (Nov 13) ([BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **1Q25** — Rabobank ~USD 10M five-year deal, the company's largest ([BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **FY2025 results (published Mar 31, 2026)**: revenue +53% yoy but 4Q25 -26% yoy; Rule of 40 turned positive (+10); cash PLN 57.7M plus 1Q26 share-issue proceeds; **sales funnel (SQL) USD 109M** (up USD 27M since Nov 2025); BOŚ flags a likely sharp drop in trailing-12M contracts signed (~USD 6–7M vs 16.1M) and stopped disclosure of that metric ([BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **2025 funding**: Series S raise of ~PLN 58M after a near-death 2023 (stock at PLN 29, going-concern warnings); stock recovered to PLN 151 by Feb 2026 (12M high 175) — BOŚ downgraded to Hold/Neutral with 12M EFV PLN 120 ([Silba](https://silbadeepdives.substack.com/p/dat-datawalk-from-wrocaw-frustration), [BOŚ Feb 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-02/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_2_GPW_6_2026_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).
- **Leadership**: Peter Sofarelli joined as VP Worldwide Sales (ex-Quantexa/SymphonyAI/SAS); positioning pivot toward "European alternative to US analytics platforms" for EU security/defense/digital-sovereignty demand ([BOŚ Mar 2026](https://bossa.pl/sites/b30/files/2026-03/document_gpw_editor/DataWalk_255_2026_AR_DM_BO%C5%9A_EN.pdf)).

## Key takeaways for Loupe

1. **No segment overlap today**: DataWalk sells $500K+/yr, services-led, single-tenant deployments to agencies and banks; it cannot serve small defense/PI practices and shows no intent to.
2. **They serve the other side of the courtroom**: DOJ prosecutors and law enforcement — a small defense practice may literally face DataWalk-powered investigations; "level the playing field" is a usable narrative.
3. **Their AI direction validates Loupe's architecture** (knowledge-graph-grounded LLMs, MCP agent access, permission-scoped agents, document-linked provenance) but is bolt-on infrastructure for analysts, not an AI-native investigator experience.
4. **White space confirmed**: no native Cellebrite/GrayKey ingestion evidence, no narrative/collection objects, no contradiction detection, no automatic narrative timelines, no self-serve motion.
5. **Watch item**: their MCP server + falling price of graph tech means a downmarket move is technically conceivable, but their 38-person headcount, enterprise price-list *increase*, and federal channel dependence make it unlikely near-term.
