- **FOIA AI assistants (May 2026 launch):** AI Reading Room Assistant (deflects requests to published records), AI Request Writing Assistant (patent pending), AI Intake Assistant (sorts by completeness/duplication/bot activity) ([Casepoint FOIA press release](https://www.casepoint.com/press/press-release-casepoint-advances-the-future-of-foia-with-next-generation-ai-powered-solution/), [PRNewswire](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/casepoint-advances-the-future-of-foia-with-next-generation-ai-powered-solution-302776222.html)). Positioned as "assistants," not autonomous agents.
- **Agentic direction (stated, not shipped as a product):** new CEO Colangelo says the roadmap emphasizes "agentic AI workflows that automate repetitive tasks within governed, auditable processes," with human-in-the-loop as the differentiator ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/casepoint-names-govtech-veteran-paul-colangelo-ceo-as-thoma-bravo-backed-company-pushes-deeper-into-government.html)).
- **Citation/provenance behavior:** explicitly **RAG "to pull answers directly from your data with full source citations"**, "transparency logs document every AI decision," data never used to train models, and all GenAI features are opt-in ([gen-AI page](https://www.casepoint.com/generative-ai-solution/)). Analyst framing: "transparent, auditable AI-assisted workflows designed for real-world scrutiny" ([eDiscovery Journal brief](https://ediscoveryjournal.com/edj-brief-casepoint-2/)).
- **March 2026 platform release** added GenAI natural-language search/summarization/classification platform-wide plus audio/video transcription ([Casepoint press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-advances-platform-unification-to-drive-efficiency-and-reduce-handoffs/)).

**Flag:** I could not verify per-answer citation UX details (pinpoint cites vs. document-level links) beyond the marketing claim of "full source citations."

## 3. Entity/Relationship Analysis

Casepoint is fundamentally **document search/review centric**, but in **March 2026** it added **"relationship visualization between persons of interest"** to eDiscovery/Investigations, and the analyst brief lists "relationship mapping" among new capabilities ([Casepoint press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-advances-platform-unification-to-drive-efficiency-and-reduce-handoffs/), [eDiscovery Journal](https://ediscoveryjournal.com/edj-brief-casepoint-2/)). **No evidence found** of a persistent knowledge graph, cross-source entity resolution, or deep communication-network analytics of the Nuix/Brainspace class — the feature appears to be a review-analytics visualization, not a graph data model. **Flag: unverified depth** — no technical documentation of the relationship-mapping feature was publicly accessible.

## 4. Evidence Types; Mobile Phone Extraction

- Documents/email: 600+ file types, Microsoft 365 collection; **Slack and Microsoft Teams direct-collection integrations** for short-message data; cloud-repository connectors; HR-system connectors and Apple-format support added 2026 ([eDiscovery software page](https://www.casepoint.com/solutions/ediscovery-software/), [March 2026 press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-advances-platform-unification-to-drive-efficiency-and-reduce-handoffs/), [eDiscovery Journal](https://ediscoveryjournal.com/edj-brief-casepoint-2/)).
- Audio/video: transcription added in the March 2026 release ([Casepoint press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-advances-platform-unification-to-drive-efficiency-and-reduce-handoffs/)).
- **Mobile phone extraction: NOT VERIFIED — likely a gap.** I found **no Casepoint page, press release, or press coverage describing Cellebrite UFED ingestion, a Physical Analyzer/Legalview-style integration, or native mobile-extraction handling**. Site-restricted and general searches for "Casepoint + Cellebrite/mobile data/text messages" returned nothing relevant (contrast: Cellebrite has shipped named integrations with **RelativityOne** — [Cellebrite/Relativity announcement](https://markets.financialcontent.com/concordmonitor/article/gnwcq-2024-11-20-cellebrite-and-relativity-deliver-solutions-to-transform-the-mobile-data-collection-landscape)). Casepoint presumably ingests UFED report exports as generic files/load files, but that is my inference, not a verified capability. This is a meaningful differentiation opening for a phone-extraction-native platform.

## 5. Chronology/Timeline/Narrative Features

**No dedicated fact/chronology/storybuilding product found.** Searches for Casepoint timeline/chronology tools surfaced only competitors' products — Everlaw Storybuilder, DISCO Timelines, Nextpoint Case Chronology, RelativityOne Timeline Builder ([Everlaw](https://support.everlaw.com/hc/en-us/articles/42469701435803-Storybuilder-Fact-Timelines), [DISCO](https://csdisco.com/offerings/timelines)). Casepoint's eDiscovery page references generic timeline widgets within review analytics, but nothing like a cited narrative/fact-management workspace ([eDiscovery software page](https://www.casepoint.com/solutions/ediscovery-software/)). **Flag: absence-of-evidence finding**, not a confirmed absence — but it did not appear in any 2025–2026 release notes I reviewed. Another opening for an AI-native chronology-with-citations product.

## 6. Deployment

- **100% cloud SaaS** — no on-prem option mentioned anywhere I checked ([eDiscovery software page](https://www.casepoint.com/solutions/ediscovery-software/)).
- **FedRAMP Moderate (2020, SEC-sponsored)** → **FedRAMP High + DOD IL4 (April 2025)** ([Casepoint press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-platform-achieves-fedramp-high-security-authorization-adds-dod-il4-designation/), [2020 PR](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/casepoint-achieves-fedramp-authorization-for-cloud-based-ediscovery-solution-for-government-301068807.html)).
- **DOD IL5 and IL6** — claims to be "one of six providers in the world" with IL6, alongside AWS, Google, Microsoft, Oracle, Palantir; Pentagon coverage adds IL5/IL6 (and mentions IL7 ambitions) for classified legal workloads ([FOIA press release](https://www.casepoint.com/press/press-release-casepoint-advances-the-future-of-foia-with-next-generation-ai-powered-solution/), [DefenseScoop](https://defensescoop.com/2026/06/24/pentagon-casepoint-ai-products-enhance-classified-legal-ops/)). CMMC Level 2 also noted ([eDiscovery Journal](https://ediscoveryjournal.com/edj-brief-casepoint-2/)). **Flag:** the IL7 mention comes only from the DefenseScoop summary; treat with caution.

## 7. Pricing / GTM

- **No published pricing.** The pricing page pitches a "Transparent, All-Inclusive Pricing Model" ("If you don't use a product, you don't pay for it") but everything routes to contact-sales/demo; no per-GB/per-user rates, tiers, minimums, or free trial are disclosed ([casepoint.com/pricing](https://www.casepoint.com/pricing/)).
- GTM is **direct enterprise/government sales** plus channel partners; flagship deals are multi-year agency contracts (SEC 5-year extension; USAF 5-year; Pentagon $98.8M BPA) ([SEC press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-achieves-fedramp-authorization-marketwatch/), [USAF press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/us-department-air-force-signs-five-year-contract-for-casepoint-ai-saas-platform/), [DefenseScoop](https://defensescoop.com/2026/06/24/pentagon-casepoint-ai-products-enhance-classified-legal-ops/)).
- **Realistic for a small criminal-defense or PI firm? No.** Target customers are federal agencies, Fortune 500 legal departments, and mid-to-large firms (Kramer Levin, Beveridge & Diamond, Lewis Roca, Larkin Hoffman); expansion focus is state/local **government**, not small firms ([law-firms page](https://www.casepoint.com/solutions/law-firms/), [LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/casepoint-names-govtech-veteran-paul-colangelo-ceo-as-thoma-bravo-backed-company-pushes-deeper-into-government.html)). **Flag:** absolute price floors are unverified (not published), but the consultative model and customer roster imply enterprise-level minimums.

## 8. Defense-Side vs. Plaintiff/Prosecution Usage

Usage skews heavily to **government/enforcement-side and agency legal ops**: SEC enforcement/investigations/litigation ([SEC press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-achieves-fedramp-authorization-marketwatch/)); USAF JAG Corps litigation, investigations, FOIA, and **congressional inquiries** ([USAF press](https://www.casepoint.com/press/us-department-air-force-signs-five-year-contract-for-casepoint-ai-saas-platform/)); DOD Office of General Counsel, DISA OGC, and 28 DLSA offices ([DefenseScoop](https://defensescoop.com/2026/06/24/pentagon-casepoint-ai-products-enhance-classified-legal-ops/)); Thoma Bravo describes both companies as "trusted partners to nearly all of the largest federal agencies" ([eDiscovery Today](https://ediscoverytoday.com/2025/01/27/opexus-and-casepoint-announce-merger-and-majority-investment-from-thoma-bravo/)). Private-side use is corporate/defense-firm civil litigation and internal investigations (Marriott, AmLaw-type firms) ([law-firms page](https://www.casepoint.com/solutions/law-firms/)). **I found no evidence of DOJ as a named customer** (DOJ appears only as the recipient of FOIA reporting formats), **and no evidence of criminal-defense-bar or PI-firm adoption** — both flagged as unverified/not found.

## 9. Notable 2025–2026 News

- **Jan 27, 2025** — OPEXUS + Casepoint merger; Thoma Bravo majority investment; combined "Casepoint + OPEXUS" entity, D.C. HQ ([Casepoint](https://www.casepoint.com/press/opexus-casepoint-merger-and-majority-investment-from-thoma-bravo/), [GovTech](https://www.govtech.com/biz/opexus-buys-casepoint-and-wins-big-pe-investment)).
- **Apr 2025** — FedRAMP High authorization + DOD IL4 ([Casepoint](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-platform-achieves-fedramp-high-security-authorization-adds-dod-il4-designation/)); IL5/IL6 followed (claimed "one of six" IL6 SaaS globally).
- **Jul 9, 2025** — USAF 5-year enterprise contract (JAG Corps, ~4,400 users, 100+ locations) ([Casepoint](https://www.casepoint.com/press/us-department-air-force-signs-five-year-contract-for-casepoint-ai-saas-platform/)).
- **Jul 18, 2025** — Acquired **mLINQS** (federal relocation-expense compliance, 40+ agencies) ([Casepoint](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-opexus-acquires-mlinqs-expanding-regulatory-and-compliance/)).
- **~Sep 2025** — CEO Howard Langsam out; interim co-CEO period ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/casepoint-names-govtech-veteran-paul-colangelo-ceo-as-thoma-bravo-backed-company-pushes-deeper-into-government.html)).
- **Mar 9, 2026** — Platform unification release: legal hold overhaul, GenAI search/summarization, A/V transcription, relationship visualization, FOIA AI ([Casepoint](https://www.casepoint.com/press/casepoint-advances-platform-unification-to-drive-efficiency-and-reduce-handoffs/)).
- **May 19, 2026** — Next-gen AI FOIA assistants launch ([Casepoint](https://www.casepoint.com/press/press-release-casepoint-advances-the-future-of-foia-with-next-generation-ai-powered-solution/)).
- **Jun 8, 2026** — **Paul Colangelo named CEO**; strategy = deeper government (incl. state/local), agentic-AI-with-audit-trails; no rebrand — still "Casepoint" ([eDiscovery Today](https://ediscoverytoday.com/2026/06/08/casepoint-appoints-paul-colangelo-as-chief-executive-officer/), [LawSites interview](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/on-lawnext-new-casepoint-ceo-paul-colangelo-on-ai-govtech-and-the-road-ahead.html)).
- **Jun 24, 2026** — Pentagon sole-award BPA up to **$98.8M** for AI-powered eDiscovery at IL5/IL6 ([DefenseScoop](https://defensescoop.com/2026/06/24/pentagon-casepoint-ai-products-enhance-classified-legal-ops/)).
- *Historical footnote:* a 2023 ALPHV/BlackCat ransomware **claim** of a Casepoint breach circulated ([Cybernews](https://cybernews.com/news/casepoint-data-breach-expose-google-meta/)); I did not verify the outcome.

## Competitive Takeaways for an AI-Native Investigative Evidence Platform

Casepoint is a government-first, FedRAMP-High/IL6 document-discovery incumbent with credible RAG-cited GenAI Q&A and strong compliance moats — but it shows **no verified mobile/Cellebrite extraction ingestion, no knowledge-graph/entity-resolution layer (only a new relationship-visualization widget), no cited chronology/storybuilding product, and no small-firm-accessible pricing**. Those four gaps map directly onto the differentiators of a phone-extraction-native, graph-and-timeline-centric platform priced for small defense/PI firms.
---
# 5. Legal AI & case-narrative tools

## 5.1 Harvey + Hebbia

# Competitive Research: Harvey AI vs Hebbia (as of mid-2026)

---

## Product 1: Harvey AI (harvey.ai)

### 1. Description, funding, scale, segment
Harvey is a domain-specific generative AI platform for legal and professional services (law firms, in-house legal, tax/professional services), founded 2022, built initially for large law firms. Latest round: **$200M growth round at an $11B valuation, closed March 25, 2026, co-led by GIC and Sequoia** with a16z, Coatue, Conviction, Elad Gil, Evantic, and Kleiner Perkins participating; over **$1B raised cumulatively** ([harvey.ai blog](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-raises-at-dollar11-billion-valuation-to-scale-agents-across-law-firms-and-enterprises)). Prior 2025 rounds: $300M Series D at $3B (Feb 2025, Sequoia-led) and $300M Series E at $5B (June 2025, Kleiner Perkins + Coatue) ([TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/06/23/four-months-after-a-3b-valuation-harvey-ai-grows-to-5b/), [Harvey blog](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-raises-series-e)). Scale: at the March 2026 raise, 1,300+ organizations, 100,000+ lawyers, majority of the AmLaw 100, 500+ in-house teams, 50 asset managers, 60+ countries; third-party trackers report **~$190–195M ARR end-2025 growing to ~$300M ARR by May 2026** and 1,500+ customers / 142,000+ lawyers ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/harvey/), [ValueAddVC](https://valueaddvc.com/blog/harvey-ai-valuation-revenue-2026-legal-ai-11b)) — ARR figures are third-party estimates, not company-audited.

### 2. Current AI capabilities (2025–2026)
Four core products: **Assistant** (chat/drafting/doc analysis), **Vault** (bulk cross-document review with tabular "review tables"), **Knowledge** (legal/regulatory research with citations), and **Workflow Agents** (multi-step automation without code) ([GC AI review](https://gc.ai/blog/harvey-legal-ai-review)). May 2026: launched **500+ pre-built practice-specific agents plus an Agent Builder** self-service customization tool (early access) ([Law.com](https://www.law.com/legaltechnews/2026/05/05/harvey-launches-pre-built-ai-agents-self-service-customization-tool/), [Harvey: Introducing Agents](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/introducing-harvey-agents)); 25,000+ custom agents already deployed per the March 2026 raise post. Chronology building is a documented first-class use case: Assistant+Vault generates fully cited case chronologies as sortable review tables (example: 5 fields × 800 emails = 4,000 data points), with sort-by-date and filter-by-sender ([Harvey In Practice: chronology from emails](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-in-practice-build-a-case-chronology-from-emails), [Academy: Case Document Timeline](https://academy.harvey.ai/use-cases-case-document-timeline)); it can also render **interactive timelines, entity charts, and dashboards** from unstructured input ([Visualize Legal Data](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/visualize-legal-data-in-harvey)). Citation behavior: outputs are citation-backed with direct links to source documents for verification; however a competitor review notes Harvey does **not** emphasize character-level/pincite-precision citation the way some rivals do ([GC AI review](https://gc.ai/blog/harvey-legal-ai-review)). I could not verify pincite granularity from primary sources — flagged.

### 3. Persistent structured case model?
**No true entity/relationship case model.** Harvey's durable objects are: Vault projects (document stores), **review tables** (persistent, queryable extraction grids you can re-query later), knowledge bases, saved agents/workflows, and Shared Spaces for collaboration ([Vault platform page](https://www.harvey.ai/platform/vault), [11B raise post](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-raises-at-dollar11-billion-valuation-to-scale-agents-across-law-firms-and-enterprises)). Chronologies/timelines are generated artifacts (tables/visualizations), not a persistent graph of entities and events; each analysis is RAG/agent runs over the vault corpus. User curation persists at the level of review tables, columns, vault folders, and sharing permissions — but there is no documented durable "significance tag"/highlight layer or entity-relationship store. Flag: Harvey does not publicly document its retrieval internals, so "no persistent case graph" is inferred from the absence of any such claim across product pages and docs.

### 4. Evidence scale and types
Per-vault storage **100,000 files**; **10,000 files per Vault project / review table / standard Assistant thread** (15,000 in Shared Space threads); file size limit raised from 100MB to **500MB** for major types ([help.harvey.ai: upload limits](https://help.harvey.ai/release-notes/increased-file-upload-limits), [Vault help](https://eu.help.harvey.ai/articles/vault), [Vault engineering blog](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/scaling-harveys-document-systems-vault-file-upload-and-management) — the latter cites M&A deals uploading 50,000+ docs in waves). **Audio supported**: upload to Assistant or Vault, auto-transcription to editable/queryable Word docs with speaker labels and timestamps; M4A/MP3/WAV/WebM/FLAC/OGG, up to 2 hours per file; deposition-transcript analysis workflow exists ([help.harvey.ai: new file types](https://help.harvey.ai/release-notes/support-for-new-file-types)). **No Cellebrite/phone-extraction (UFDR) support found anywhere** — flagged as absent. Video support not confirmed. Multi-gigabyte discovery is handled only as bulk document sets via Vault; no e-discovery-style processing (deduplication, load files) is documented.

### 5. Deployment and confidentiality
SOC 2 Type II + ISO 27001 (annually renewed); **zero-retention on by default**, never trains on customer data unless opt-in; customer-selectable retention windows including zero-day; encryption at rest/in transit. Deployment choices: **multi-tenant with isolation, single-tenant, or private VPC/VNet**, with US/EU/UK processing regions for data residency ([harvey.ai/security](https://www.harvey.ai/security), [trust.harvey.ai](https://trust.harvey.ai/)). No on-prem (fully air-gapped) option documented.

### 6. Pricing/GTM
No public pricing; demo-gated enterprise sales, no self-serve or free trial. Peer-reported figures: **~$1,200/user/month (up to ~$2,400/seat with Lexis integration), commonly reported 20–25+ seat minimums, 12-month terms**; annual contracts typically $50K–$300K+; effective entry point ~$360K/yr at minimums; per-seat drops to ~$100–200/month at AmLaw-100 volume ([GC AI review](https://gc.ai/blog/harvey-legal-ai-review), [thelegalprompts pricing analysis](https://thelegalprompts.com/blog/harvey-ai-pricing), [costbench](https://costbench.com/software/ai-legal-tools/harvey-ai/), [AI Vortex](https://www.aivortex.io/legal/ai-tools/harvey-ai-pricing-2026/)). Small firms effectively cannot buy: "no self-serve signup, no free trial, no SMB pricing" ([AI Vortex small-firm assessment](https://www.aivortex.io/legal/ai-tools/harvey-ai-for-small-firms/)). All seat figures are reported/leaked, not official — flagged.

### 7. Criminal-defense-specific usage
**No criminal-defense-specific product.** Harvey markets a general litigation solution (discovery organization, searching thousands of communications, citation-backed findings, privilege flagging, chronology assembly) ([harvey.ai/solutions/litigation](https://www.harvey.ai/solutions/litigation), [How Litigation Teams Use Harvey](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-in-practice-how-litigation-teams-use-harvey)). Third-party commentary says criminal defense attorneys use general legal AI for discovery review/motions, but I found no Harvey-published criminal-defense case study or feature — flagged as unverified for Harvey specifically.

### 8. Notable 2025–2026 news
- Feb 2025: $300M Series D at $3B (Sequoia) ([TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/06/23/four-months-after-a-3b-valuation-harvey-ai-grows-to-5b/)).
- June 2025: $300M Series E at $5B (Kleiner Perkins + Coatue); expansion into tax/professional services ([Harvey blog](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-raises-series-e), [Fortune](https://fortune.com/2025/06/23/harvey-raises-300-million-at-5-billion-valuation-to-be-legal-ai-for-lawyers-worldwide/)).
- March 2026: $200M at **$11B** (GIC + Sequoia); firmwide wins incl. NBCUniversal, HSBC, DLA Piper International ([Harvey blog](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/harvey-raises-at-dollar11-billion-valuation-to-scale-agents-across-law-firms-and-enterprises)).
- May 2026: 500+ pre-built agents + Agent Builder launch ([Law.com](https://www.law.com/legaltechnews/2026/05/05/harvey-launches-pre-built-ai-agents-self-service-customization-tool/)); ~$300M ARR reported ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/harvey/)).
- Ongoing product cadence via monthly "The Brief" posts (data visualization, mobile/voice-to-prompt, Shared Spaces) ([The Brief: May 2026](https://www.harvey.ai/blog/the-brief-may-2026)). A reported LexisNexis integration surfaces in seat-pricing reports; I hit the search budget before verifying the partnership's primary source — flagged.

---

## Product 2: Hebbia (hebbia.ai)

### 1. Description, funding, scale, segment
Hebbia is an enterprise AI document-analysis platform ("Matrix") founded 2020 by George Sivulka, targeting **financial services first (asset managers, PE, credit, banks) and Big Law second**. Funding: **$161M total; last priced round remains the $130M Series B at ~$700M valuation, led by a16z, July 2024** — no Series C found as of July 2026 ([TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2024/07/09/ai-startup-hebbia-rased-130m-at-a-700m-valuation-on-13-million-of-profitable-revenue), [Hebbia blog](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/hebbia-raises-usd130m-series-b), [PitchBook](https://pitchbook.com/profiles/company/439260-40), [Premier Alts: still $700M as of Feb 2026](https://www.premieralts.com/companies/hebbia/valuation)). Scale: $13M ARR at the Series B (profitable); third-party claims of ~$30M ARR in 2024 and ~$24.6M ARR (Getlatka) are **unverified and inconsistent** — flagged ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/hebbia/), [Getlatka](https://getlatka.com/companies/hebbia.com)). Customers: ~33–40% of top global asset managers by AUM, 50+ Fortune 500s, law firms Latham & Watkins, Cleary Gottlieb, Ropes & Gray, Seyfarth; also US Air Force ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/hebbia/), [hebbia.com/industry/law](https://www.hebbia.com/industry/law)).

### 2. Current AI capabilities (2025–2026)
Core product **Matrix**: a tabular data-grid where each cell is an LLM agent run — documents as rows, prompts as columns — built on a multi-agent architecture ("iterative source decomposition") that decomposes questions and retrieves without chunking constraints; Hebbia claims this "nearly eliminated tool-use hallucinations" ([Multi-Agent Redesign blog](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/divide-and-conquer-hebbias-multi-agent-redesign), [Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/hebbia/)). Also Chat/Deep Research, and shareable **Agents**: users build one-click repeatable workflows published to an org library; a **Global Agent Library** of hundreds of pre-built finance/legal automations; agents can "run continuously, triggering workflows" proactively; plus an **Email Agent ("Intern")** that works from the inbox and returns completed work with citations ([What's New Aug 2025](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/the-disclosure-august-2025), [April 2026 Disclosure](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/whats-new-april-disclosure-2026), [hebbia.com/product](https://www.hebbia.com/product)). Citations/provenance: marketing promises "complete traceability back to every finding" with source-linked answers; per OpenAI's case study Hebbia's system cites sources for extracted answers ([OpenAI case study](https://openai.com/index/hebbia/)). Pincite granularity not documented — flagged. **Chronology/timeline building is not a marketed capability** — no timeline feature found; a chronology could only be assembled manually via Matrix columns (date extraction + sort) — flagged as absent.

### 3. Persistent structured case model?
**No entity/relationship case model.** Durable objects are **Projects** (shared spaces where teams "publish Chats and Matrices" with inherited permissions, project-level custom instructions, and an activity tab), saved **Matrix grids** (which as of April 2026 can themselves be data sources for Chat and for other Matrices — grid outputs are durable, composable objects with a "Workflow View" dependency graph), and shared Agents in an org library ([April 2026 Disclosure](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/whats-new-april-disclosure-2026), [product page](https://www.hebbia.com/product)). This is closer to a persistent structured layer than Harvey's (grids feeding grids ≈ durable derived data), but it is document/answer-centric, not an entity-and-relationship store. No highlight/significance-tag layer documented. Underlying Q&A remains agentic retrieval over the corpus per run.

### 4. Evidence scale and types
Matrix handles "thousands of rows and dozens of columns" per grid and claims reasoning over "an effectively infinite number of documents"; Matrix Chat upgraded to handle thousands of rows (April 2026) ([Multi-Agent Redesign](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/divide-and-conquer-hebbias-multi-agent-redesign), [April 2026 Disclosure](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/whats-new-april-disclosure-2026)). **No published hard doc-count or GB limits** — flagged. **Audio/video**: bulk upload + transcription of up to 100 files at a time, ~60 languages, then source-linked querying ([Hebbia resource page](https://www.hebbia.com/resources/best-ai-for-document-analysis)). Advanced parsing of tables, charts, and scanned documents (April 2026). **No Cellebrite/phone-extraction support found** — flagged as absent. No e-discovery processing pipeline documented; positioning is deal/diligence data rooms, not multi-gigabyte litigation discovery.

### 5. Deployment and confidentiality
**SOC 2 Type II**, ISO certifications including **ISO/IEC 42001:2023** (AI management), GDPR; AES-256 at rest, TLS 1.3 in transit; "never trains on your data" ([hebbia.com/security](https://www.hebbia.com/security), [hebbia.com/industry/law](https://www.hebbia.com/industry/law)). Third-party reporting says single-tenant/specialized deployments are available at higher cost on custom contracts ([AI Agent Square pricing](https://aiagentsquare.com/blog/hebbia-pricing)); Hebbia's own site does not enumerate deployment models or LLM zero-retention arrangements with model providers — **both flagged as unverified from primary sources** (details live behind trust.hebbia.ai).

### 6. Pricing/GTM
No public pricing, no self-serve tier; enterprise-gated custom contracts scoped to seats/usage/security/deployment. Reported: **~$10,000/seat/year for Professional (builder) seats; $3,000–3,500/seat/year for Lite (consumer) seats**; land-and-expand into PE/credit/M&A teams ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/hebbia/), [AI Agent Square](https://aiagentsquare.com/blog/hebbia-pricing), [Metronome pricing index](https://metronome.com/pricing-index/hebbia)). Effectively enterprise-only; small firms cannot buy self-serve. Seat figures are third-party reported — flagged.

### 7. Criminal-defense-specific usage
**None found.** Hebbia's legal page targets transactional Big Law work — banking/finance documentation, due diligence, capital markets, PE fund formation, "matter intelligence" — with no litigation, e-discovery, or criminal-defense offering on its own site ([hebbia.com/industry/law](https://www.hebbia.com/industry/law)). Some third-party blogs mention "e-discovery for litigation" as a Hebbia use ([eesel](https://www.eesel.ai/blog/hebbia-ai)) but this is not corroborated by Hebbia's own materials — flagged.

### 8. Notable 2025–2026 news
- **May 2025: acquired FlashDocs** (presentation/document generation), feeding the presentation-template and deck-generation features ([PitchBook](https://pitchbook.com/profiles/company/439260-40), [Tracxn](https://tracxn.com/d/companies/hebbia/__QVC5BFqOGZ0XKXpKNqSBMDNtp5MbZKx6B_v8_KNqpVE)).
- 2025: shareable org-library Agents; OpenAI showcase claiming Hebbia agents automate "90% of finance and legal work," multi-model stack (o1 for reasoning, GPT-4o general) ([OpenAI case study](https://openai.com/index/hebbia/), [Aug 2025 Disclosure](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/the-disclosure-august-2025)).
- Dec 2025 / April 2026 Disclosures: Email Agent "Intern," Global Agent Library, Company Screening + Precedent Transactions skills (CapIQ/FactSet), Matrix-as-data-source composability, Workflow View, GPT-5.4 integration, model-agnostic routing ([Dec 2025](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/the-disclosure-december-2025), [April 2026](https://www.hebbia.com/blog/whats-new-april-disclosure-2026)).
- Notably **no new funding round since July 2024** despite the sector's 2025–26 capital surge (Harvey $11B, Legora $5.5B) ([PlatinumIDS sector overview](https://blog.platinumids.com/blog/legal-ai-billion-dollar-arms-race-2026)).

---

## Cross-cutting flags (could not verify)
- **Neither product has any documented Cellebrite/UFDR/phone-extraction ingestion** — a clear white space vs. forensic-extraction-native tooling.
- Neither builds a persistent entity/relationship/timeline graph; both persist grids/tables + document stores; timelines (Harvey) are generated artifacts, and Hebbia has no timeline feature at all.
- All ARR and seat-price figures for both companies are third-party/peer-reported, not audited disclosures; Hebbia's post-2024 ARR is especially murky ($13M verified June 2024; $24.6M–$30M claims conflict).
- Harvey pincite precision, the Harvey–LexisNexis partnership primary source, and Hebbia's VPC/on-prem and LLM zero-retention arrangements were not verifiable from primary sources before the search budget was exhausted.
---
## 5.2 CoCounsel + Clearbrief

# Competitive Research: CoCounsel (Thomson Reuters) and Clearbrief — as of July 2026

---

## Product 1: CoCounsel (Thomson Reuters, formerly Casetext)

### 1. Description
CoCounsel is Thomson Reuters' flagship legal AI assistant, originally built by Casetext (founded 2013) as the first GPT-4-powered "AI legal assistant" delivering document review, research memos, deposition prep, and contract analysis. Thomson Reuters signed a definitive agreement to acquire Casetext for **$650M cash on June 26, 2023**, closing **August 17, 2023** ([TR press release](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en/press-releases/2023/june/thomson-reuters-corporation-signs-definitive-agreement-to-acquire-casetext), [close](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en/press-releases/2023/august/thomson-reuters-completes-acquisition-of-casetext-inc), [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2023/06/26/thomson-reuters-buys-casetext-an-ai-legal-tech-startup-for-650m-in-cash/)). At acquisition Casetext served 10,000+ law firms and legal departments. Now the AI layer across TR's legal franchise, it reasons over ~1.9 billion Westlaw and Practical Law documents ([intellectia](https://intellectia.ai/news/stock/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-ai-integration-with-claude)). Target segment: the full spectrum from AmLaw firms to solos, corporate legal, courts, and government — sold as an add-on/companion to Westlaw ([product page](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/en/products/cocounsel-legal)).

### 2. Current AI capabilities (2025–2026)
- **CoCounsel Legal launched Aug 5, 2025** as an agentic platform: guided workflows that "plan, reason and execute complex multi-step workflows" (drafting complaints, employee/privacy policies, discovery requests/responses, deposition transcript reviews) plus **Deep Research** — billed as the industry's first professional-grade agentic research, generating a reviewable multi-step research plan before execution, grounded in Westlaw KeyCite/Key Numbers ([LawSites Aug 2025](https://www.lawnext.com/2025/08/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-with-agentic-ai-and-deep-research-capabilities-along-with-a-new-and-final-version-of-westlaw.html)).
- **Next-generation CoCounsel Legal, rebuilt on Anthropic's Claude Agent SDK**: early access opened week of **June 22, 2026**; GA planned **August 2026** (US), then Canada/UK/Australia. Moves from discrete "skills" to an agent that drafts a plan from a plain-language matter description, pulls firm precedents + Westlaw/Practical Law, and iterates a single work product. Coming: **Brief Builder** (agentic brief/motion drafting with cite-checking) and a firm-intelligence skill-authoring layer ([LawSites June 2026](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/thomson-reuters-opens-early-access-to-the-next-generation-of-cocounsel-legal-saying-beta-users-fing-loved-the-product.html)).
- Classic skills remain: Review Documents (word-for-word review with per-document/per-question answer tables), Compare Documents, **Timeline** (chronologies from medical records, transcripts, notes; can be scoped to a topic/timeframe; results are *downloaded* and manually pruned) ([Timeline help](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/help/cocounsel/legal/skills/skills-prompts-workflows/timeline), [Review help](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/content/helpandsupp/en-us/help/cocounsel/legal/skills/understanding-cocounsel-skills/review-documents.html)). Timeline shipped as a skill back in Dec 2023 ([Dewey B Strategic](https://www.deweybstrategic.com/2023/12/casetext-cocounsel-launches-timeline-feature.html)).
- **MCP integration with Anthropic** lets lawyers working in Claude reach CoCounsel Legal directly ([intellectia](https://intellectia.ai/news/stock/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-ai-integration-with-claude)).
- Provenance model: citation-grounding to Westlaw/Practical Law sources with visible reasoning; Lawyerist still cautions attorneys must verify citations and conclusions ([Lawyerist review](https://lawyerist.com/reviews/artificial-intelligence-in-law-firms/cocounsel-review-artificial-intelligence-for-lawyers/)).

### 3. Persistent structured case model?
Historically **no** — skills were per-run, context-window-shaped jobs producing downloadable outputs (the Timeline help page implies download-and-edit, not a durable in-app chronology object). The 2026 rebuild introduces **Workspaces**: "dedicated environments carrying documents, precedents, and positions across sessions and team members," and the product page says workspaces "keep your documents, templates, and prior work organized by matter… pick up exactly where you left off, with reasoning that reflects the full history" ([LawSites June 2026](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/thomson-reuters-opens-early-access-to-the-next-generation-of-cocounsel-legal-saying-beta-users-fing-loved-the-product.html), [product page](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/en/products/cocounsel-legal)). This is matter-scoped document/output persistence — **there is no evidence of a durable structured case graph (entities, relationships, event objects) or of user judgment (highlights, significance tags, curated collections) persisting as first-class objects**. DMS integration is federated search, not full indexing ([LawSites Aug 2025](https://www.lawnext.com/2025/08/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-with-agentic-ai-and-deep-research-capabilities-along-with-a-new-and-final-version-of-westlaw.html)). *Flag: workspace internals are new and thinly documented; depth of persistence unverified.*

### 4. Evidence scale and types
- Per-document cap: **2,000 pages / 100MB per document** (help center guidance; larger documents must be split) ([Compare Documents help](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/help/cocounsel/legal/skills/skills-prompts-workflows/compare-documents) and related TR help pages surfaced in search). Compare Documents: ~**200 documents per run**, with quality degrading at high counts (explicitly: 100-doc comparison "far less granular" than 5) ([same page](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/help/cocounsel/legal/skills/skills-prompts-workflows/compare-documents)).
- Review Documents per-run document-count limit: **not verified** — TR help pages found do not publish it. *Flag: unverified.*
- **No documented multimedia (audio/video) analysis, no phone-extraction (Cellebrite/UFDR) support, and no documented multi-gigabyte discovery-database handling** in any source reviewed. Inputs are document-centric (PDF/Word/transcripts/medical records). Timeline help warns large sets produce extraneous entries needing manual cleanup ([Timeline help](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/help/cocounsel/legal/skills/skills-prompts-workflows/timeline)). *Flag: absence-of-evidence; TR may have unpublicized capabilities.*

### 5. Deployment and confidentiality
Multi-tenant SaaS; no on-prem/single-tenant option documented. **SOC 2 Type II and ISO 27001**; **zero-retention API calls to LLM providers**, with OpenAI/Google (and per the Claude partnership, Anthropic) contractually barred from training on customer data; TLS 1.2 in transit, AES-256 at rest; LLM processing in the US ([TR blog](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/blog/built-for-law-trusted-by-leaders-the-thomson-reuters-ai-edge/), [TR security help](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/help/cocounsel/tax-audit-accounting/security/security-info-cocounsel-tax-audit-accounting), [gov legal blog](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/blog/the-two-non-negotiables-of-secure-legal-ai/)). TR also launched a "Trust in AI Alliance" with Anthropic, Google, OpenAI, AWS ([TR blog](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/blog/built-for-law-trusted-by-leaders-the-thomson-reuters-ai-edge/)).

### 6. Pricing/GTM
TR publishes **no clean price sheet**; pricing is negotiated and bundled with Westlaw. Third-party 2026 estimates: **CoCounsel Core ~$4,500/user/yr (~$225/mo, document work only, no Westlaw)**; **+Practical Law ~$6,600/user/yr**; **+Westlaw ~$9,000/user/yr**; tier names now "CoCounsel Essentials," "Westlaw Advantage with CoCounsel Essentials" (~$639/user/mo), "Practical Law Dynamic Tool Set with CoCounsel Essentials," "CoCounsel Legal"; overall range reported ~$104–$639/user/mo ([Spellbook breakdown](https://spellbook.com/learn/cocounsel-pricing), [costbench](https://costbench.com/software/ai-legal-tools/cocounsel/), [AI Vortex](https://www.aivortex.io/legal/compare/cocounsel-pricing-2026/), [lawxyai](https://www.lawxyai.com/articles/cocounsel-pricing-review-2026-real-costs-lawyers-miss)). Accessible to solos in principle but Lawyerist flags it as expensive and add-on-positioned ([Lawyerist](https://lawyerist.com/reviews/artificial-intelligence-in-law-firms/cocounsel-review-artificial-intelligence-for-lawyers/)). *Flag: all dollar figures are third-party estimates, not TR-published.*

### 7. Criminal defense
TR runs a dedicated **criminal-defense marketing page**: discovery-set summarization ("cut review 63%"), charging/arrest/complaint timelines ("79% faster"), deposition prep, pretrial motion drafting, "**75+ prebuilt criminal defense workflows**," federal sentencing guidelines/grand-jury content ([TR criminal law page](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/en/legal/criminal-law)). Aimed at private criminal defense attorneys; **no dedicated public-defender or DA-office program, and no bodycam/phone-dump/multimedia evidence handling, is documented**. TR separately markets CoCounsel Legal to courts ([courts blog](https://legal.thomsonreuters.com/blog/responsible-ai-in-courts-the-answer-is-cocounsel-legal/)). *Flag: PD/DA program absence unverified beyond marketing pages.*

### 8. Notable 2025–2026 news
- **Aug 5, 2025**: CoCounsel Legal launch with agentic AI + Deep Research, alongside **Westlaw Advantage** — billed as the "final" named version of Westlaw with continuous updates ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2025/08/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-with-agentic-ai-and-deep-research-capabilities-along-with-a-new-and-final-version-of-westlaw.html), [Barchart/PR](https://www.barchart.com/story/news/33887210/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-transforming-legal-work-with-agentic-ai-and-deep-research)).
- **June 2026**: Next-gen CoCounsel Legal on **Claude Agent SDK**, early access June 22, GA Aug 2026; Workspaces, Brief Builder ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/06/thomson-reuters-opens-early-access-to-the-next-generation-of-cocounsel-legal-saying-beta-users-fing-loved-the-product.html)).
- **2026**: TR–Anthropic **MCP integration** connecting Claude to CoCounsel Legal ([intellectia](https://intellectia.ai/news/stock/thomson-reuters-launches-cocounsel-legal-ai-integration-with-claude)); commentary on concentration/reliance risk of the TR–Anthropic pairing ([CCB Journal](https://ccbjournal.com/blog/thomson-reuters-claude-and-the-legal-ai-reliance-problem)).

---

## Product 2: Clearbrief

### 1. Description
Clearbrief (Seattle, founded **2020** by **Jacqueline Schafer**, ex-Paul Weiss litigator and former assistant attorney general in Alaska and Washington) is a **Microsoft Word add-in** that grounds litigation drafting in the evidentiary record: every factual assertion and citation is hyperlinked to the exact source page, with AI cite-checking, timelines, and transcript summaries ([Geek Law Blog interview](https://www.geeklawblog.com/2023/10/jacqueline-schafer-on-writing-briefs-at-the-speed-of-ai-how-clearbrief-is-transforming-legal-drafting.html), [Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/clearbrief/)). Funding is small and murky: Tracxn reports **~$8.19M total across 3 rounds** (incl. a $4M raise filed March 2024), while GetLatka claims it is bootstrapped at **~$3.6M ARR, ~33 employees, $10.9M valuation** — *these conflict; treat exact funding/revenue as unverified* ([Tracxn](https://tracxn.com/d/companies/clearbrief/__QOk9c5sQEYs6xG2DJ94Yn4Q29AVdKdAYs93O4l9mPrM), [GetLatka](https://getlatka.com/companies/clearbrief.com), [Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/clearbrief/)). Scale claim: **3.2M+ pleadings drafted since 2021**; customers span AmLaw 10/200 firms, in-house teams, state/federal courts, arbitrators, and government agencies ([clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/)). Target segment: litigation only — explicitly not transactional/contract work ([AI Vortex](https://www.aivortex.io/legal/compare/clearbrief-pricing-2026/)).

### 2. Current AI capabilities (2025–2026)
- **Cite-checking**: cross-references legal citations against verified databases (via a **LexisNexis integration**) to catch fabricated/inaccurate cites; produces a downloadable **Cite Check Report** as an audit trail — positioned squarely at the hallucinated-citation liability problem ([Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/clearbrief/), [clearbrief.com/press](https://clearbrief.com/press)).
- **Record cite-to-evidence**: "Add fact cite" suggests supporting evidence from the uploaded record for each sentence; cited source renders in an adjacent pane for instant verification ([Geek Law Blog](https://www.geeklawblog.com/2023/10/jacqueline-schafer-on-writing-briefs-at-the-speed-of-ai-how-clearbrief-is-transforming-legal-drafting.html)).
- **Hyperlinked timelines/chronologies**: extracts dates/events from case documents into a Word table (date, description, hyperlinked record cite), generated in seconds ([LawSites 2023 exclusive](https://www.lawnext.com/2023/08/exclusive-new-ai-features-in-clearbrief-create-hyperlinked-timelines-and-allow-users-to-query-their-documents.html), [demo video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9wpO5mbvKTE)).
- **Document Q&A** over uploaded documents; **deposition/hearing transcript summaries** (table or narrative); **"Verified Facts"** statement-of-facts generation from e-discovery documents with linked sources; tables of authorities/exhibit tables ([LawSites Dec 2023](https://www.lawnext.com/2023/12/clearbrief-adds-another-ai-feature-verified-facts-to-create-statements-of-facts-plus-a-wearable-ai-brooch/), [AAA use-case guide](https://go.adr.org/rs/294-SFS-516/images/Clearbrief_use_cases.pdf), [clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/)).
- Provenance is Clearbrief's core identity — outputs are only ever assertions linked to record pages, not free-standing generation.

### 3. Persistent structured case model?
Partially, and document-centric rather than entity-centric. Cases live as matter workspaces of uploaded source documents that persist across sessions and drafts (users "selectively import relevant materials" from repositories of thousands to tens of thousands of documents — [Geek Law Blog](https://www.geeklawblog.com/2023/10/jacqueline-schafer-on-writing-briefs-at-the-speed-of-ai-how-clearbrief-is-transforming-legal-drafting.html)). Timelines and cite links are exportable Word artifacts tied to that record. **No evidence of a persistent structured case model in the entity/relationship/graph sense** (no people/phone/account objects, no relationship mapping), and **no documented durable user-judgment layer** (significance tags, curated evidence collections as first-class persistent objects) beyond the document set and generated tables. *Flag: internal persistence architecture unverified; based on public materials only.*

### 4. Evidence scale and types
Inputs: pleadings, briefs, exhibits, deposition/hearing transcripts, medical records, e-discovery documents ([AAA guide](https://go.adr.org/rs/294-SFS-516/images/Clearbrief_use_cases.pdf), [Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/clearbrief/)). Founder describes users on matters of **thousands to tens of thousands of documents**, but with **selective import** — it is not an e-discovery platform ingesting full multi-GB productions ([Geek Law Blog](https://www.geeklawblog.com/2023/10/jacqueline-schafer-on-writing-briefs-at-the-speed-of-ai-how-clearbrief-is-transforming-legal-drafting.html)). **No published file-size/document-count limits found** (*flag: unverified*). **No audio/video/multimedia analysis and no phone-extraction (Cellebrite/UFDR) support documented anywhere.** Text/PDF-record-centric by design.

### 5. Deployment and confidentiality
Cloud SaaS delivered through the Microsoft Word add-in ecosystem; **SOC 2 Type II**; customer data never used to train LLMs; an enterprise **"Bring Your Own Storage"** option lets organizations keep documents in their own cloud — notable for confidentiality-sensitive buyers, though not full on-prem ([clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/), [Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/clearbrief/)). *Flag: which LLM providers Clearbrief uses, and their retention terms, not publicly documented in sources reviewed.*

### 6. Pricing/GTM
Conflicting figures: Clearbrief's own site and SoftwareFinder cite **$300/user/month for Solo & Small Teams** (unlimited use, cite verification, chronology generation) ([clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/), [SoftwareFinder](https://softwarefinder.com/legal/clearbrief)); AI Vortex reports quote-based **~$150–350/user/month** by firm size, ~10–15% annual discounts, and occasional per-brief deals ([AI Vortex](https://www.aivortex.io/legal/compare/clearbrief-pricing-2026/)). Enterprise is custom with volume discounts and training. No seat minimums documented. Materially cheaper than CoCounsel's Westlaw-bundled tiers and self-serve enough for solos/small firms, but $300/mo is still a real hurdle for solo criminal defense. GTM channels: Word add-in marketplace motion plus partnerships (LexisNexis, MyCase case management, AAA, law firms like Dykema) ([clearbrief.com/press](https://clearbrief.com/press)).

### 7. Criminal defense
No dedicated criminal-defense product or PD/DA program documented. Adjacent signals: founder's public-sector DNA (state AG offices, child-welfare/asylum litigation) and stated adoption by "courts and government agencies" ([Geek Law Blog](https://www.geeklawblog.com/2023/10/jacqueline-schafer-on-writing-briefs-at-the-speed-of-ai-how-clearbrief-is-transforming-legal-drafting.html)); court/judge-facing tooling for verifying parties' record citations ([clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/)). *Flag: no verified public-defender or prosecutor deployments found in this research.*

### 8. Notable 2025–2026 news
- **Legalweek 2026 "Litigation Technology of the Year"** award; also ranked highest in a Nevada State Bar AI evaluation ([clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/)). 2025 Legalweek Leaders in Tech finalist (multiple categories) and LegalTech Breakthrough "Legal Compliance Solution of the Year" ([press page](https://clearbrief.com/press)).
- **American Arbitration Association (AAA-ICDR) partnership** — Clearbrief provided to its **5,500 arbitrators and mediators** ([press page](https://clearbrief.com/press), [AAA guide](https://go.adr.org/rs/294-SFS-516/images/Clearbrief_use_cases.pdf)).
- **LexisNexis** citation-linking integration, **MyCase** integration, **Dykema** firm-wide adoption ([press page](https://clearbrief.com/press)).
- Deeply embedded in the **Microsoft Word ecosystem** as its primary surface ([clearbrief.com](https://clearbrief.com/), [sandbox](https://sandbox.clearbrief.com/)). *Flag: no formal Microsoft corporate partnership announcement verified.*

---

## Cross-cutting takeaways (relevant to Loupe positioning)
- **Neither product builds a durable structured case model** (entity/relationship graph, persistent curated evidence objects, cross-session investigative state). CoCounsel's 2026 "Workspaces" is the closest move and is brand-new; Clearbrief persists document sets and cite links, not entities.
- **Neither handles phone extractions, audio/video, or multi-gigabyte forensic discovery** — the evidence types at the center of criminal defense. CoCounsel's criminal-defense play is workflow templates over ordinary documents.
- Both are hard-capped by per-run document limits (CoCounsel: 2,000 pp/100MB per doc, ~200 docs per compare run) or selective-import workflows (Clearbrief), i.e., context-window-shaped, not corpus-shaped.
- Pricing: CoCounsel ~$225–639/user/mo (bundled, opaque); Clearbrief ~$150–350/user/mo — both per-seat SaaS with no on-prem, only Clearbrief offering BYO-storage.

**Unverified items flagged inline**: CoCounsel Review-skill per-run doc count; workspace persistence depth; PD/DA program absence; Clearbrief funding total (Tracxn vs GetLatka conflict), revenue/valuation figures, file limits, LLM provider terms, and any Microsoft/court formal partnerships beyond those listed.
---
## 5.3 CaseMap (LexisNexis) + Litera

# Competitive Research: CaseMap Suite & Litera One/AI (as of July 2026)

**Critical ownership correction (affects the whole brief):** The premise that Litera owns the CaseMap suite is **wrong**. CaseMap, TimeMap, and TextMap were built by CaseSoft and acquired by **LexisNexis** in 2006, and remain LexisNexis products today ([Crunchbase](https://www.crunchbase.com/acquisition/lexisnexis-acquires-casesoft--4aeceb04), [Wikipedia: CaseMap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CaseMap), [LexisNexis product page](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)). Litera (the Hg Capital-backed company) is a separate vendor; its own litigation fact-management product, **Litera Litigate** (ex-Allegory), was **discontinued in 2024** ([Litera Litigate page](https://www.litera.com/products/litera-litigate), [LawSites on the 2020 acquisition](https://www.lawnext.com/2020/08/litera-acquires-litigation-management-platform-allegory-law-from-integreon.html)). Both are covered below, correctly attributed.

---

## Section A: CaseMap Suite / CaseMap+ AI (LexisNexis — NOT Litera)

### 1. Description, ownership, scale, segment
CaseMap is the classic litigation case-analysis database: a relational workspace for facts, issues, documents, and entities, launched in 1998 as a Microsoft Access app by a Florida attorney, sold by CaseSoft, acquired by LexisNexis in 2006 ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CaseMap), [Crunchbase](https://www.crunchbase.com/acquisition/lexisnexis-acquires-casesoft--4aeceb04)). The suite comprises CaseMap (facts/issues analysis), TimeMap (visual timelines), TextMap (transcript/deposition management), and Sanction (trial presentation) ([LexisNexis suite page](https://law.lexisnexis.com/casemap-2)). A cloud version launched 2022; it was relaunched as **CaseMap+ AI** in March 2025 ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2025/03/casemap-the-lexisnexis-case-management-platform-gets-gen-ai-for-summaries-timelines-and-more.html)). Target segment: litigation teams from small firms to 500+-lawyer firms, corporate legal, litigation service providers, and government agencies including prosecutors and Attorneys General ([LexisNexis product page](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page), [Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/casemap-ai-by-lexisnexis/)). No user/revenue scale figures published (unverified).

### 2. Current AI capabilities (2025–2026)
CaseMap+ AI (announced 2025-03-04) added generative AI across the suite ([GlobeNewswire release](https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2025/03/04/3036416/0/en/LexisNexis-Unveils-CaseMap-AI-Transforming-Litigation-Case-Management-and-Analysis-with-AI-Powered-Solutions.html), [Artificial Lawyer](https://www.artificiallawyer.com/2025/03/04/lexisnexis-launches-casemap-ai-for-timelines-transcripts-more/)):
- **Deposition/transcript summarization** — AI topical summaries with interactive topic lists, **convertible into case facts** ([Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/casemap-ai-by-lexisnexis/), [LexisNexis](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)).
- **Document summarization** — auto-populates the document synopsis field as documents are uploaded.
- **Auto-create timelines/chronologies** — AI constructs chronologies from facts and converts to visual aids (the TimeMap function, absorbed into the platform).
- **Issue organization** — develops hierarchies of claims/counterclaims.
- **Transcript search with synced video** (TextMap lineage).
- LexisNexis' stated vision is to integrate **Protégé**, its personalized AI assistant, into CaseMap+ AI for analyze/strategize/draft workflows (CEO Sean Fitzpatrick quote, [LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2025/03/casemap-the-lexisnexis-case-management-platform-gets-gen-ai-for-summaries-timelines-and-more.html)).
- **Provenance:** LexisNexis says its AI approach uses RAG grounded in trusted content ([GlobeNewswire](https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2025/03/04/3036416/0/en/LexisNexis-Unveils-CaseMap-AI-Transforming-Litigation-Case-Management-and-Analysis-with-AI-Powered-Solutions.html)), but **no published detail on per-fact citation/provenance behavior of the AI outputs** — unverified beyond the summary→fact conversion flow. No standalone "chat with your evidence" document Q&A is documented for CaseMap+ (unverified; Protégé integration is "vision" stage).

### 3. Persistent structured case model
This is CaseMap's defining strength: a relational database of **facts, issues, documents, physical evidence, depositions, pleadings, and "objects" (persons, organizations, places)**, with users linking evidence sources to facts and facts to legal issues; the fact table sorts chronologically into timelines ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CaseMap)). The cloud platform keeps this as a "centralized workspace" for cases, facts, documents, issues, and entities with multi-user simultaneous editing, version control, and user-level tracking, plus evaluation reports and heatmaps ([LexisNexis](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)). **AI now does populate the model**: deposition summaries are convertible into facts, document summaries auto-fill synopsis fields, and AI builds chronologies and issue hierarchies ([Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/casemap-ai-by-lexisnexis/)) — i.e., AI-assisted fact extraction feeding durable objects, with human conversion in the loop. Investigator judgment persists as durable objects (fact evaluations, issue links, annotations) — this has always been the product's core. Competitor Casefleet notes "AI-proposed facts" exist only in the cloud CaseMap+ AI, not legacy desktop ([Casefleet comparison — competitor source, treat with care](https://www.casefleet.com/comparisons/casemap-alternative)).

### 4. Evidence scale and types
- Supports **98 file formats** (PDF, DOCX, XLSX, MSG, EML, PST, transcript formats) ([LexisNexis](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)).
- **Transcripts:** TextMap lineage gives searchable, annotatable depositions with **synchronized video** ([Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/casemap-ai-by-lexisnexis/)).
- **Discovery-scale ingest:** via "Send to CaseMap" integrations with **RelativityOne and iManage**, retaining tags/coding — the intended path for large e-discovery volumes ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2025/03/casemap-the-lexisnexis-case-management-platform-gets-gen-ai-for-summaries-timelines-and-more.html)). User reviews complain of slow document uploads and slow performance on large cases ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/35995/CaseMap/), [Casefleet](https://www.casefleet.com/comparisons/casemap-alternative)).
- **Phone-extraction support (Cellebrite/UFED-type):** **no evidence of any native support found** — nothing in product pages, releases, or reviews. Flagged: absence verified only as "not documented."
- **Hard database size limits:** not published anywhere I could verify (legacy desktop file-based limits are unverified — flag).
- Cloud has web + iOS/Android apps; legacy desktop is Windows-only ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/35995/CaseMap/), [Casefleet](https://www.casefleet.com/comparisons/casemap-alternative)).

### 5. Deployment and confidentiality
CaseMap+ AI is **cloud/SaaS**; the legacy Windows desktop suite remains available in "legacy support" mode (kept for users needing offline use; new development is cloud-only since 2022) ([SoftwareAdvice](https://www.softwareadvice.com/legal/casemap-profile/), [Casefleet](https://www.casefleet.com/comparisons/casemap-alternative)). Desktop cases import into the cloud preserving facts and issues ([LexisNexis](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)). Security posture: encryption, enterprise cloud infrastructure, third-party audits, and a commitment that **customer data is never used to train AI models** ([LexisNexis](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)). **Single-tenant/private-cloud options: not documented — unverified.** For maximum confidentiality, the legacy desktop remains the only fully local option.

### 6. Pricing / GTM
No published pricing — "contact vendor," quote-based, monthly subscriptions available; free trial offered via the suite page ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/35995/CaseMap/), [SoftwareAdvice](https://www.softwareadvice.com/legal/casemap-profile/), [LexisNexis suite page](https://law.lexisnexis.com/casemap-2)). Reviews call pricing "steep relative to value" and note opaque pricing; competitor Casefleet contrasts its own public $30/user/mo pricing against CaseMap's sales-led model ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/35995/CaseMap/), [Casefleet](https://www.casefleet.com/comparisons/casemap-alternative)). Reviews suggest it suits firms with staff support; solo/small practices struggle with implementation complexity ([SoftwareAdvice](https://www.softwareadvice.com/legal/casemap-profile/)). Small-firm accessibility: weak.

### 7. Criminal-defense usage
LexisNexis explicitly targets **government agencies — prosecutors, Attorneys General, federal/state** — as CaseMap customers ([LexisNexis product page](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)). That is the only sourced criminal-justice segment claim I could verify. **Unverified/not found:** any public-defender program, discounted criminal-defense licensing, or named prosecutor/defender deployments in 2025–2026 coverage (historical DOJ usage is anecdotal and I found no citable source within budget — flag). Legaltech Hub's segment list for CaseMap+ AI notably omits criminal/government ([Legaltech Hub](https://www.legaltechnologyhub.com/vendors/casemap-ai-by-lexisnexis/)).

### 8. Notable 2025–2026 news
- **2025-03-04:** CaseMap+ AI launch (gen-AI summaries, timelines, issue hierarchies; Protégé integration roadmap) ([GlobeNewswire](https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2025/03/04/3036416/0/en/LexisNexis-Unveils-CaseMap-AI-Transforming-Litigation-Case-Management-and-Analysis-with-AI-Powered-Solutions.html), [Law.com](https://www.law.com/legaltechnews/2025/03/04/lexisnexis-announces-release-of-updated-case-management-platform-casemap-ai/)).
- Legacy desktop CaseMap/TimeMap/TextMap effectively **feature-frozen** ("support but no further updates" per competitor analysis; no announced end-of-life date) ([Casefleet](https://www.casefleet.com/comparisons/casemap-alternative)) — flag: sunset framing comes from a competitor.
- National Law Journal "Best Case Management Software" 2025 + 2026 Hall of Fame ([LexisNexis](https://www.lexisnexis.com/en-us/products/casemap.page)).
- Cloud-migration backlash is a live theme: 3.7/5 rating, polarized between desktop loyalists and cloud users reporting freezing, broken links, data-loss concerns ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/35995/CaseMap/), [SoftwareAdvice](https://www.softwareadvice.com/legal/casemap-profile/)).

---

## Section B: Litera One / Litera AI (Lito)

### 1. Description, ownership, scale, segment
Litera is a Chicago-based legal-tech company (founded 1995, Workshare lineage) selling drafting, comparison, transaction, contract-AI, and firm-intelligence software, assembled through ~18 acquisitions (Kira 2021, Allegory 2020, FileTrail 2024, Office & Dragons 2024, Peppermint Technology Feb 2025) ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litera_(company)), [Tracxn](https://tracxn.com/d/acquisitions/acquisitions-by-litera/__5tiTZFzpttuje9upgSAwu8ZL-OOIqjnWqbOCtWc6NNQ), [LawSites Allegory](https://www.lawnext.com/2020/08/litera-acquires-litigation-management-platform-allegory-law-from-integreon.html)). **Hg Capital has backed Litera since 2019**; the July 2026 relaunch was self-funded with no new capital ([LawSites relaunch coverage](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/07/litera-relaunches-its-brand-around-a-unified-vision-one-ai-agent-spanning-the-practice-and-business-of-law.html)). Scale: **15,000+ customers, 1M daily users, 10M document comparisons/month, 99% of the Am Law 100, 99% logo retention, ~1,050 employees** ([Litera relaunch release](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-relaunches-unite-practice-and-business-law), [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litera_(company))). Target segment: BigLaw/mid-law transactional and firm-operations — decisively **not** litigation fact-management (see #3).

### 2. Current AI capabilities (2025–2026)
- **Lito**, Litera's agentic AI, went GA **2025-10-15** (early access late Aug 2025), formally launched 2025-10-27, embedded in Litera One (Web + Outlook) ([Litera announcement](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-introduces-lito-next-evolution-agentic-ai-legal-fully-built-your-firms-trusted-experience), [Legal IT Insider](https://legaltechnology.com/2025/10/27/litera-gets-into-the-agentic-ai-game-with-formal-lito-launch/), [Artificial Lawyer](https://www.artificiallawyer.com/2025/10/27/litera-rolls-out-lito-agent-to-boost-productivity/)).
- Capabilities: **Chat with Doc Viewer** (document Q&A in-viewer), **Analyze in Grid** (multi-doc × multi-prompt matrix with validation), **Compare** (deterministic redlining, 1:1/1:many/bulk, Word/PDF/Excel/PowerPoint), natural-language **search over Foundation firm data**, **Review Terms**, and "Legal Skills" including form checks, **timeline generation**, and open-items lists ([Litera announcement](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-introduces-lito-next-evolution-agentic-ai-legal-fully-built-your-firms-trusted-experience)). Litera claims a baseline library of **11 legal AI skills** spanning Capital Markets, M&A, BD, **Litigation**, and Compliance ([BusinessWire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251027431790/en/Litera-Democratizes-AI-for-Legal-Professionals-in-its-Core-Products-Giving-Millions-of-Lawyers-No-Cost-Access-to-Advanced-Agentic-AI-Lito)) — the litigation skills are not itemized anywhere I found (flag).
- **Kira** (contract AI) adds GenAI on 1,400+ lawyer-trained smart fields, Generative Smart Fields (no-training-data extraction), Smart Summaries, and a document chat feature; claimed 90% accuracy ([Litera Kira page](https://www.litera.com/products/kira)).
- **Provenance/citation behavior:** Litera's differentiator claim is hybrid **deterministic rules-based engines + LLMs** ("we don't get the luxury of being 80% good"), with Lito calling deterministic tools for high-accuracy work ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/07/litera-relaunches-its-brand-around-a-unified-vision-one-ai-agent-spanning-the-practice-and-business-of-law.html)). Per-answer citation/provenance mechanics and underlying LLM vendors are **not disclosed** (flag: unverified).

### 3. Persistent structured case model
**Litera has none for litigation anymore.** Its fact-chronology product Litera Litigate (ex-Allegory: chronologies, cast of characters, deposition/exhibit management, hot-doc ingest from e-discovery) was **discontinued per 2024 notifications** ([Litera Litigate page](https://www.litera.com/products/litera-litigate), [LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2020/08/litera-acquires-litigation-management-platform-allegory-law-from-integreon.html), [ABA Journal on the fact-management market](https://www.abajournal.com/columns/article/you-have-options-when-it-comes-to-litigation-fact-management-software)). What persists structurally today is transactional/firm-level: Kira's extracted contract data points and **Foundation 365** (institutional knowledge + CRM on Microsoft Dynamics 365, at 5 of the 10 largest global firms) ([LawSites relaunch](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/07/litera-relaunches-its-brand-around-a-unified-vision-one-ai-agent-spanning-the-practice-and-business-of-law.html)). Lito's timeline-generation skill produces outputs, but there is **no evidence of a durable facts/issues/evidence object model** that investigator judgment persists into — a genuine competitive gap versus CaseMap (and versus a fact-first platform like ours).

### 4. Evidence scale and types
Litera's platform is document-centric (Word/PDF/Excel/PowerPoint comparison at 10M/month scale; Kira contract analysis at scale; Analyze in Grid for multi-document batches) ([Litera relaunch](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-relaunches-unite-practice-and-business-law), [Kira page](https://www.litera.com/products/kira)). **No transcript-management, multimedia, phone-extraction, or discovery-scale evidence-database capability is documented** — those lived in the discontinued Litigate product. No size limits published. Flag: absence of evidence, not verified absence.

### 5. Deployment and confidentiality
Cloud/SaaS-first, natively integrated with Microsoft 365 and Google Workspace; Litera One runs on web, Word, Outlook, PC/Mac/mobile with DMS integrations (iManage, NetDocuments, SharePoint, OneDrive) ([Litera One blog](https://www.litera.com/blog/litera-one-2025-redefining-legal-collaboration-ai)). **Kira** documents SOC 2 Type II/SOC 3, GDPR/DORA/NIS2 alignment, data residency in US/Canada/Europe/APAC, "flexible hosting including on-premises support," and no training on customer data without consent ([Kira page](https://www.litera.com/products/kira)). Lito claims enterprise-grade infrastructure and connects only to structured, **permissioned** firm data ([Lito announcement](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-introduces-lito-next-evolution-agentic-ai-legal-fully-built-your-firms-trusted-experience)). Single-tenant options for Lito/Litera One specifically: **not documented — unverified**.

### 6. Pricing / GTM
Headline GTM move: **Lito is free** ("no-cost") for customers of core products — Litera Draft Base/Pro/Advanced, Kira, Foundation 365, Postilize — with "no near-term change" and possible separately-priced premium skills later ([BusinessWire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251027431790/en/Litera-Democratizes-AI-for-Legal-Professionals-in-its-Core-Products-Giving-Millions-of-Lawyers-No-Cost-Access-to-Advanced-Agentic-AI-Lito), [Legal IT Insider](https://legaltechnology.com/2025/10/27/litera-gets-into-the-agentic-ai-game-with-formal-lito-launch/), [LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/07/litera-relaunches-its-brand-around-a-unified-vision-one-ai-agent-spanning-the-practice-and-business-of-law.html)). Underlying per-seat prices for Draft/Kira are not published (quote-based; flag). GTM is enterprise/BigLaw-led (99% of Am Law 100); small-firm accessibility exists via Draft tiers but the platform is not marketed to small criminal-defense practices.

### 7. Criminal-defense-specific usage
**None found.** Litera's marketing, case studies, and the Lito/relaunch coverage target commercial transactional practice (M&A, capital markets, contracts) and firm business operations; the discontinued Litigate was aimed at complex civil litigation ([LawSites Allegory](https://www.lawnext.com/2020/08/litera-acquires-litigation-management-platform-allegory-law-from-integreon.html)). No prosecutor/defender programs or criminal-matter references located — flag as verified-absent from public materials, not conclusively absent.

### 8. Notable 2025–2026 news
- **Feb 2025:** Acquired Peppermint Technology (legal CRM/marketing, UK); Apr 2025 Harbor partnership for CX365 (CRM/ERM/matter management) ([Tracxn](https://tracxn.com/d/acquisitions/acquisitions-by-litera/__5tiTZFzpttuje9upgSAwu8ZL-OOIqjnWqbOCtWc6NNQ)); late 2024: FileTrail and Office & Dragons.
- **Spring 2025:** began consolidating its sprawling catalog into three pillars (practice / manage / grow), folding Kira and Transact in ([LawSites](https://www.lawnext.com/2026/07/litera-relaunches-its-brand-around-a-unified-vision-one-ai-agent-spanning-the-practice-and-business-of-law.html)).
- **Oct 2025:** Lito GA + "democratization" (free-with-core-products) announcement; ~68–70% of Litera One Web accounts actively using Lito within months ([BusinessWire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251027431790/en/Litera-Democratizes-AI-for-Legal-Professionals-in-its-Core-Products-Giving-Millions-of-Lawyers-No-Cost-Access-to-Advanced-Agentic-AI-Lito), [Litera](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-democratizes-ai-legal-professionals)).
- **2026-07-15:** Full brand relaunch around "one AI agent" spanning practice and business of law; ILTACON 2026 (Nashville, Aug 23–27) showcase planned ([Litera relaunch](https://www.litera.com/newslinks/litera-relaunches-unite-practice-and-business-law), [Artificial Lawyer](https://www.artificiallawyer.com/2026/07/15/litera-relaunches-with-one-agent-to-rule-the-platform/)).
- Litigate discontinuation (2024 notices) — Litera's exit from litigation fact-management ([Litera](https://www.litera.com/products/litera-litigate)).

---

## Unverified / flagged items (summary)
- The task's premise "Litera owns CaseMap / Lito descends from DocXtools AI" — **incorrect**: CaseMap is LexisNexis; Lito's lineage is Kira + Litera's rules engines.
- CaseMap: hard DB size limits, per-seat pricing, single-tenant cloud, per-fact AI citation behavior, DOJ/defender usage specifics, phone-extraction support (nothing documented).
- Litera: underlying LLM vendors for Lito, itemized "11 legal AI skills" (esp. the litigation ones), Lito citation mechanics, single-tenant Litera One, Draft/Kira per-seat pricing.
- Casefleet comparison claims (legacy CaseMap feature-freeze, review themes) come from a direct competitor.

**Competitive takeaways for Loupe:** CaseMap+ AI is the only incumbent with a durable facts/issues/entities object model that AI now feeds (via human-confirmed conversion), but it is cloud-only, sales-priced, weak on multimedia/phone-extraction evidence, and suffering a rocky desktop-to-cloud migration. Litera has exited litigation fact-management entirely — its Lito platform is a drafting/transactional agent, not an evidence platform. Neither vendor documents any phone-extraction (Cellebrite-class) ingestion or criminal-defense program — both are open lanes.
---
# 6. AI-native criminal-defense & evidence startups

# AI-Native Litigation / Evidence Startups — Competitive Research (as of July 2026)

**Method note:** Built from live web research (mid-2026). The session's web-search budget was exhausted partway through, so later verification was done by direct page fetches of known URLs; items I could not verify are explicitly flagged.

---

## 1. JusticeText — the anchor (justicetext.com)

**1. Description.** JusticeText is an AI evidence-analysis platform built *specifically for criminal defense*, founded by University of Chicago engineering students Devshi Mehrotra and Leslie Jones-Dove after the Laquan McDonald dashcam release ([Thomson Reuters Institute profile](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/posts/technology/justicetext-ai-audiovisual-analysis/)). It ingests bodycam footage, interrogation videos, jail calls, 911 audio and (since late 2025) written discovery, and turns them into searchable, time-stamped, AI-analyzed case material ([justicetext.com](https://justicetext.com)). Funding: a ~$2.2–2.5M seed backed by Bloomberg Beta, True Ventures, Reid Hoffman and Michael Tubbs, plus $100K from Google's Black Founders Fund ([Robots.net](https://robots.net/news/justicetext-empowers-public-defenders-with-ai-technology/), [JusticeText news page](https://justicetext.com/latest/)). Scale as of Dec 2025: **$4.0M ARR, 4,100+ attorneys, 7 of 22 statewide public-defender systems, 1,000+ weekly active users uploading ~3,000 hours of footage per week**, ~400 private defense attorneys ([JusticeText Substack, 12/23/25](https://justicetext.substack.com/p/justicetext-update-122325-expanding)); Thomson Reuters cites 70+ public defense agencies and 300+ private firms.

**2. AI capabilities (2025–26).** Automatic multi-language transcription (claimed 100+ languages) with time-stamped searchable transcripts; AI-generated summaries; automated flagging of legally significant moments (Miranda warnings, sobriety tests, potential constitutional violations); and **"Miranda AI"**, a legal-specific assistant that helps construct timelines, identify inconsistencies across evidence, and extract facts for defense strategy ([TR Institute](https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/posts/technology/justicetext-ai-audiovisual-analysis/), [justicetext.com](https://justicetext.com)). The Dec 2025 release added "inter-context connections" across documents and media — full-case AI analysis spanning audio, video, and PDFs ([Substack update](https://justicetext.substack.com/p/justicetext-update-122325-expanding)).

**3. Persistent case model.** Partial. User judgment persists: timestamped notes, flags, clips, and collaborative annotation are durable per case ([GovTech Kentucky piece](https://www.govtech.com/public-safety/some-kentucky-public-defenders-say-ai-can-help-defendants)); the marketing site advertises building case timelines and identifying inconsistencies across evidence. But there is **no public evidence of a durable entity/relationship graph** (people, phones, accounts as first-class linked objects) — the "connections" language reads as cross-document AI analysis rather than a persistent structured case model. *Unverified either way.*

**4. Evidence scale/types.** Core strength is A/V at real-world defense scale — Kentucky cases run from 3 to 100+ hours of bodycam per case ([GovTech](https://www.govtech.com/public-safety/some-kentucky-public-defenders-say-ai-can-help-defendants)). Multi-camera scene syncing shipped Dec 2025. Written discovery (police reports, witness statements, medical records) added late 2025. **No public support claimed for phone-extraction reports (Cellebrite/GrayKey UFDR)** — a visible white space ([Substack update](https://justicetext.substack.com/p/justicetext-update-122325-expanding)).

**5. Deployment/confidentiality.** Cloud SaaS with hands-on office rollout; a Montana case-management API does one-click discovery uploads from the state PD system. **No SOC2/CJIS certification found anywhere public** — justicetext.com/security and /faq both 404, and neither the homepage nor press mentions certifications. *Flagged unverified.*

**6. Pricing/GTM.** No public pricing. GTM is direct partnership with PD agencies (Kentucky DPA ~400 attorneys, Connecticut, Harris County, PDS-DC, many counties — [news page](https://justicetext.com/latest/)); one public datapoint: Cumberland County PA approved a **$17,300 one-year grant-funded contract** for the PD office ([CitizenPortal](https://citizenportal.ai/articles/6146769/Cumberland-County/Pennsylvania/Public-defender-to-pilot-JusticeText-discovery-review-software-under-grant-funding)). Grant-funded pilots are a recurring adoption pattern; Kentucky notes funding limits prevent statewide access ([Governing](https://www.governing.com/policy/can-ai-help-kentuckys-public-defenders)).

**7. Criminal-defense specificity.** It *is* the product — defense-only positioning, PD-first.

**8. 2025–26 news.** Expansion from bodycam-only to multimodal evidence review (Dec 2025); $4M ARR disclosure; used across 20 states ([Clio blog](https://www.clio.com/blog/criminal-defense-ai/)); NLADA training webinars on AI bodycam review ([NLADA](https://learninglab.nlada.org/products/ai-for-bodycam-review-how-public-defenders-are-challenging-police-narratives-with-video-evidence)).

---

## 2. Matey AI / CrimD (matey.ai) — closest true competitor

**1. Description.** Austin-based startup (CEO Jared White; team of engineers, investigators, and legal professionals) building **CrimD**, an AI eDiscovery/evidence platform exclusively for criminal defense. Raised **$7.5M (announced Aug 27, 2025)** led by Timespan Ventures with Neo and Streamlined Ventures ([funding release](https://markets.financialcontent.com/dowtheoryletters/article/accwirecq-2025-8-27-matey-raises-75m-to-scale-proven-legal-ai-built-for-the-real-world-of-criminal-defense)). Used by public defenders, firms, and government agencies; claims 90% time savings on discovery review and $40K+ cost savings per case.

**2. AI capabilities.** Auto-transcription of video/audio during ingestion; evidence Q&A "traceable to case evidence"; AI timeline generation "about any event in the case"; summarization; motion/trial-prep drafting; automated classification of discovery into folders; witness/connection identification; full audit trails and citation-verified outputs ([matey.ai/crimd](https://www.matey.ai/crimd), funding release).

**3. Persistent case model.** Advertises persistent case structure — tags, timelines, organized folders that endure across the case — plus audit logs. Like JusticeText, no public evidence of an entity-relationship graph; timelines appear to be generated-then-kept artifacts. *Partially unverified.*

**4.
